. Claude Code v2.1.322 2026-11-11 17:02 UTC 23m 31s $6.08 16 runs 3 in flight model sonnet judge sonnet threshold 100%
Suite score · with plugin88.2%mean of per-case scores
Ablation Δ↑ +1.3score points vs baseline, 4 of 4 cases
Baseline score86.9%without the plugin
Cases40 of 4 ≥ 100% threshold
Perfect runs25%runs where every grader passed
How to read this report
  • A run's score is the weighted fraction of its graders that passed; a "perfect" run passed every grader.
  • A case's score is the mean of its runs; a case passes when its score is at or above the 100% threshold (the tick on each case bar).
  • The suite score is the mean of the per-case scores.
  • Judge votes are independent samples of the LLM judge; the majority decides pass or fail.
  • "With plugin" and "Baseline" runs are identical except for the plugin being loaded; Δ is the with-plugin score minus the baseline score.

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
## What I assumed you need

- **R1 (must):** specific crop-type labels (wheat, maize, rapeseed and so on) at field or pixel level.
- **R2 (must):** multi-temporal Sentinel-2 data already paired with the labels.
- **R3 (must):** a licence that lets you publish results and share trained models.
- Downloadable today, no application.

## Shortlist

All rows were checked against the Zenodo or Hugging Face registry record on 2026-11-11, except the fit notes marked "recalled".

| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Fit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | EuroCropsML | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | Strong |
| 2 | BreizhCrops | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | Strong |
| 3 | TimeSen2Crop | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | Strong |
| 4 | PASTIS | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | Strong |
| 5 | AgriSen-COG | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | Strong |
| 6 | S2-Agri (Pixel-Set) | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | Strong |
| 7 | Sen4AgriNet (Hugging Face copy) | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | Partial |
| 8 | CropHarvest | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | Partial |

## Details

- **EuroCropsML** (https://zenodo.org/records/15095445)
  - It has 706,683 parcels across 176 crop classes in Estonia, Latvia and Portugal.
  - Each parcel has a median Sentinel-2 L1C time series, and there are ready-made splits.
  - The data are parcel aggregates, not image patches.
  - Paper: Nature Scientific Data 2025, doi 10.1038/s41597-025-04952-7.
- **BreizhCrops** (https://zenodo.org/records/14585341)
  - Field-level labels for Brittany, France, with Sentinel-2 top-of-atmosphere and bottom-of-atmosphere time series.
  - A GitHub repository has baselines and pretrained weights.
  - The paper compares seven deep networks and a random forest: doi 10.5194/isprs-archives-xliii-b2-2020-1545-2020.
- **TimeSen2Crop** (https://zenodo.org/records/4715631)
  - It has over 1 million labelled pixel time series covering 16 crop types over one agronomic year.
  - Paper: doi 10.1109/jstars.2021.3073965.
  - It covers one region and one year.
- **PASTIS** (https://zenodo.org/records/5012942)
  - Parcel-level labels with Sentinel-2 patch time series over France, suited to segmentation.
  - Contents are from memory.
- **AgriSen-COG** (https://zenodo.org/records/7892012)
  - A multi-country benchmark (Austria, Belgium, Spain, Denmark, the Netherlands) with labels from farmers' parcel declarations (LPIS).
  - The Zenodo record title says "LPIS.GT", so it may hold only the labels. Check where the imagery is hosted.
  - Paper: doi 10.3390/rs15122980.
- **S2-Agri Pixel-Set** (https://zenodo.org/records/5815488): Sentinel-2 pixel sets with crop labels. A Patch version is at https://zenodo.org/records/5815523. Contents are from memory.
- **Sen4AgriNet** (https://huggingface.co/datasets/isp-uv-es/sen4agrinet-taco)
  - A multi-country benchmark with LPIS labels, repackaged by a third party.
  - Confirm it against the original release before citing it.
  - Original paper: doi 10.1109/jstars.2022.3164771.
- **CropHarvest** (https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.10251170): global, with pre-computed time series. Most labels are crop versus non-crop, so it only partly meets R1.

**Licences and sharing models (R3):** CC-BY lets you publish results and share models with attribution. For the CC-BY-SA datasets (EuroCropsML, Sen4AgriNet copy, CropHarvest), share-alike clearly applies to redistributed data. Whether it applies to trained weights is unsettled, so check it with your supervisor if you plan to release models. CC-BY (BreizhCrops, TimeSen2Crop, PASTIS, AgriSen-COG, S2-Agri) avoids that question.

## Known, not confirmed

- **S4A-CyL** (Sentinel-2 time series from Castilla y León, https://doi.org/10.71486/q4yz-p373): the DOI resolves, but the registry didn't state the licence or access tier. Read the landing page first.
- **Peru Andes crop-type time series** (https://zenodo.org/records/19949051): the Zenodo record is open under CC-BY-4.0, but I didn't check its labels or crop classes.

## Not recommended, and why

- **ZueriCrop** has a CC-BY-NC-4.0 licence, so non-commercial only. That is a risk for sharing models.
- **EuroCrops** is the harmonised parcel labels without Sentinel-2 series. EuroCropsML is its paired version.

## What I could not find

Zenodo and DataCite returned truncated responses on some queries, so the list may be incomplete. I didn't read the PASTIS, AgriSen-COG or S2-Agri pages, so their contents and size are recalled. I didn't look for baseline papers for most datasets.

## Next step

Start with BreizhCrops or EuroCropsML. Both are open, small enough to try this week, and have published baselines to compare against.

- **Peru Andes crop-type time series** (https://zenodo.org/records/19949051): the Zenodo record is open under CC-BY-4.0, but I didn't check its labels or crop classes.

## Not recommended, and why

- **ZueriCrop** has a CC-BY-NC-4.0 licence, so non-commercial only. That is a risk for sharing models.
- **EuroCrops** is the harmonised parcel labels without Sentinel-2 series. EuroCropsML is its paired version.

## What I could not find

Zenodo and DataCite returned truncated responses on some queries, so the list may be incomplete. I didn't read the PASTIS, AgriSen-COG or S2-Agri pages, so their contents and size are recalled. I didn't look for baseline papers for most datasets.

## Next step

Start with BreizhCrops or EuroCropsML. Both are open, small enough to try this week, and have published baselines to compare against.

## Details

- **EuroCropsML** (https://zenodo.org/records/15095445)
  - It has 706,683 parcels across 176 crop classes in Estonia, Latvia and Portugal.
  - Each parcel has a median Sentinel-2 L1C time series, and there are ready-made splits.
  - The data are parcel aggregates, not image patches.
  - Paper: Nature Scientific Data 2025, doi 10.1038/s41597-025-04952-7.
- **BreizhCrops** (https://zenodo.org/records/14585341)
  - Field-level labels for Brittany, France, with Sentinel-2 top-of-atmosphere and bottom-of-atmosphere time series.
  - A GitHub repository has baselines and pretrained weights.
  - The paper compares seven deep networks and a random forest: doi 10.5194/isprs-archives-xliii-b2-2020-1545-2020.
- **TimeSen2Crop** (https://zenodo.org/records/4715631)
  - It has over 1 million labelled pixel time series covering 16 crop types over one agronomic year.
  - Paper: doi 10.1109/jstars.2021.3073965.
  - It covers one region and one year.
- **PASTIS** (https://zenodo.org/records/5012942)
  - Parcel-level labels with Sentinel-2 patch time series over France, suited to segmentation.
  - Contents are from memory.
- **AgriSen-COG** (https://zenodo.org/records/7892012)
  - A multi-country benchmark (Austria, Belgium, Spain, Denmark, the Netherlands) with labels from farmers' parcel declarations (LPIS).
  - The Zenodo record title says "LPIS.GT", so it may hold only the labels. Check where the imagery is hosted.
  - Paper: doi 10.3390/rs15122980.
- **S2-Agri Pixel-Set** (https://zenodo.org/records/5815488): Sentinel-2 pixel sets with crop labels. A Patch version is at https://zenodo.org/records/5815523. Contents are from memory.
- **Sen4AgriNet** (https://huggingface.co/datasets/isp-uv-es/sen4agrinet-taco)
  - A multi-country benchmark with LPIS labels, repackaged by a third party.
  - Confirm it against the original release before citing it.
  - Original paper: doi 10.1109/jstars.2022.3164771.
- **CropHarvest** (https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.10251170): global, with pre-computed time series. Most labels are crop versus non-crop, so it only partly meets R1.

**Licences and sharing models (R3):** CC-BY lets you publish results and share models with attribution. For the CC-BY-SA datasets (EuroCropsML, Sen4AgriNet copy, CropHarvest), share-alike clearly applies to redistributed data. Whether it applies to trained weights is unsettled, so check it with your supervisor if you plan to release models. CC-BY (BreizhCrops, TimeSen2Crop, PASTIS, AgriSen-COG, S2-Agri) avoids that question.

## Known, not confirmed

- **S4A-CyL** (Sentinel-2 time series from Castilla y León, https://doi.org/10.71486/q4yz-p373): the DOI resolves, but the registry didn't state the licence or access tier. Read the landing page first.
- **Peru Andes crop-type time series** (https://zenodo.org/records/19949051): the Zenodo record is open under CC-BY-4.0, but I didn't check its labels or crop classes.

## What I assumed you need

- **R1 (must):** Crop-specific labels (wheat, maize, rapeseed and so on) per parcel or pixel.
- **R2 (must):** Sentinel-2 time series already paired with the labels and ready to use.
- **R3 (must):** A licence that lets you publish results and share trained models.
- **R4 (nice to have):** An established benchmark with published baselines.

I assumed a European or global setting is fine. I checked on 12 Nov 2026.

## Shortlist

| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | PASTIS | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | 28.8 GB | Strong | Record today; class details from memory |
| 2 | BreizhCrops | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | 7.7 GB | Strong | Today |
| 3 | EuroCropsML | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | 4.8 GB | Strong | Today |
| 4 | S2-Agri (Pixel-Set; Patch also available) | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | 10.8 GB | Strong | Record today; contents from memory |
| 5 | Sen4AgriNet (Hugging Face) | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | Large, not stated | Strong | Record today; contents from memory |
| 6 | AgriSen-COG | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | 28 GB | Partial | Today |
| 7 | CropHarvest | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | Not stated | Partial | Record today; contents from memory |

All seven records exist and their licences come from the registry records. "Today" means the registry record was read. "From memory" means I recalled the content details (classes, regions) and did not re-read them.

## Details

- **PASTIS** (https://zenodo.org/records/5012942):
  - It holds S2 time-series patches over French agricultural parcels with crop-type labels.
  - From memory, it has 18 classes.
  - It is the standard benchmark for parcel segmentation. You can derive per-parcel classification from it.
- **BreizhCrops** (https://zenodo.org/records/14585341):
  - It has per-parcel S2 time series (top-of-atmosphere and bottom-of-atmosphere) for Brittany.
  - The paper compares seven deep networks and a Random Forest baseline.
  - Loaders and pretrained weights are at github.com/dl4sits/breizhcrops, as named in the paper abstract.
  - It covers one region and one year.
  - Paper: doi 10.5194/isprs-archives-xliii-b2-2020-1545-2020.
- **EuroCropsML** (https://zenodo.org/records/15095445):
  - It has 706,683 labelled parcels over 176 crop classes in Latvia, Portugal and Estonia, with per-parcel median S2 L1C series for 2021.
  - It has the most classes of any option here.
  - Papers: doi 10.1038/s41597-025-04952-7 (dataset paper) and doi 10.1016/j.ophoto.2026.100117 (benchmark study).
- **S2-Agri** (https://zenodo.org/records/5815488; patch version https://zenodo.org/records/5815523):
  - It is parcel-based crop classification with S2 series.
  - The record text is brief. From memory it covers France and one season with about 20 classes, so read the related article first.
- **Sen4AgriNet** (https://huggingface.co/datasets/isp-uv-es/sen4agrinet-taco):
  - From memory, it is a very large multi-year dataset with about 168 declared-crop classes, covering Catalonia and France. Read the dataset card before downloading.
- **AgriSen-COG** (https://zenodo.org/records/7892012):
  - This record holds the rasterised LPIS ground-truth tiles.
  - I did not confirm whether the paired S2 imagery is in this record or a separate one.
- **CropHarvest** (DOI 10.5281/zenodo.10251170):
  - It has pre-extracted satellite time series, but most labels are crop versus non-crop. A smaller multi-class subset exists, from memory.
  - It is only a partial fit for your multi-class goal.

**Licences and your goal of publishing results and sharing models:**
- **CC-BY-4.0** (PASTIS, BreizhCrops, S2-Agri, AgriSen-COG): attribution only, so there are no problems.
- **CC-BY-SA-4.0** (EuroCropsML, Sen4AgriNet, CropHarvest): share-alike applies to the data and to datasets derived from it. Publishing results and models is normally fine. If you plan to release a derived dataset, it must carry the same licence. Check this with your supervisor.

## Known, not confirmed

- **TimeSen2Crop** (Austria, about 1M labelled S2 samples, 16 crop classes from memory):
  - The paper DOI resolves (10.1109/JSTARS.2021.3073965) and OpenAIRE lists CC-BY-4.0.
  - I did not find the data record itself, so check the paper's data link.
- **Peru, multi-annual S2 crop dataset** (https://zenodo.org/records/19949051): CC-BY-4.0 and verified, but a single district. It is only useful as a small extra test region.

## Not recommended, and why

- **ZueriCrop:** the Hugging Face copy is CC-BY-NC-4.0. I could not confirm the original terms, and the non-commercial clause may block sharing models.
- **IBM/NASA multi-temporal crop classification:** it uses Harmonized Landsat-Sentinel imagery with US CDL labels, not pure S2.
- **EuroCrops (labels only):** it has no imagery. EuroCropsML is the version with S2 attached.
- **National crop maps for Germany (Zenodo 6451848):** these are derived maps, access is by application, and no licence is stated.

## What I could not find

- TimeMatch, DENETHOR, Munich480, AI4Boundaries and SICKLE returned nothing usable from the registries, so I did not evaluate them.
- The Datacite and Zenodo searches failed intermittently, so the list may be incomplete.
- The literature lookup for PASTIS returned unrelated papers, so I list no paper for PASTIS, S2-Agri, Sen4AgriNet or CropHarvest.

## What I assumed you need

- **R2 (must):** Sentinel-2 time series already paired with the labels and ready to use.

I assumed a European or global setting is fine. I checked on 12 Nov 2026.

## Shortlist

| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | PASTIS | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | 28.8 GB | Strong | Record today; class details from memory |
| 2 | BreizhCrops | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | 7.7 GB | Strong | Today |
| 3 | EuroCropsML | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | 4.8 GB | Strong | Today |

All seven records exist and their licences come from the registry records. "Today" means the registry record was read. "From memory" means I recalled the content details (classes, regions) and did not re-read them.

## Details

- **PASTIS** (https://zenodo.org/records/5012942):
  - It holds S2 time-series patches over French agricultural parcels with crop-type labels.
  - From memory, it has 18 classes.
  - It is the standard benchmark for parcel segmentation. You can derive per-parcel classification from it.
- **BreizhCrops** (https://zenodo.org/records/14585341):
  - It has per-parcel S2 time series (top-of-atmosphere and bottom-of-atmosphere) for Brittany.
  - The paper compares seven deep networks and a Random Forest baseline.
  - Loaders and pretrained weights are at github.com/dl4sits/breizhcrops, as named in the paper abstract.
  - It covers one region and one year.
  - Paper: doi 10.5194/isprs-archives-xliii-b2-2020-1545-2020.
- **EuroCropsML** (https://zenodo.org/records/15095445):
  - It has 706,683 labelled parcels over 176 crop classes in Latvia, Portugal and Estonia, with per-parcel median S2 L1C series for 2021.
  - It has the most classes of any option here.
  - Papers: doi 10.1038/s41597-025-04952-7 (dataset paper) and doi 10.1016/j.ophoto.2026.100117 (benchmark study).
- **S2-Agri** (https://zenodo.org/records/5815488; patch version https://zenodo.org/records/5815523):
  - It is parcel-based crop classification with S2 series.
  - The record text is brief. From memory it covers France and one season with about 20 classes, so read the related article first.
- **Sen4AgriNet** (https://huggingface.co/datasets/isp-uv-es/sen4agrinet-taco):
  - From memory, it is a very large multi-year dataset with about 168 declared-crop classes, covering Catalonia and France. Read the dataset card before downloading.
- **AgriSen-COG** (https://zenodo.org/records/7892012):
  - This record holds the rasterised LPIS ground-truth tiles.
  - I did not confirm whether the paired S2 imagery is in this record or a separate one.
- **CropHarvest** (DOI 10.5281/zenodo.10251170):
  - It has pre-extracted satellite time series, but most labels are crop versus non-crop. A smaller multi-class subset exists, from memory.
  - It is only a partial fit for your multi-class goal.

**Licences and your goal of publishing results and sharing models:**
- **CC-BY-4.0** (PASTIS, BreizhCrops, S2-Agri, AgriSen-COG): attribution only, so there are no problems.
- **CC-BY-SA-4.0** (EuroCropsML, Sen4AgriNet, CropHarvest): share-alike applies to the data and to datasets derived from it. Publishing results and models is normally fine. If you plan to release a derived dataset, it must carry the same licence. Check this with your supervisor.

## Known, not confirmed

- **TimeSen2Crop** (Austria, about 1M labelled S2 samples, 16 crop classes from memory):
  - The paper DOI resolves (10.1109/JSTARS.2021.3073965) and OpenAIRE lists CC-BY-4.0.
  - I did not find the data record itself, so check the paper's data link.
- **Peru, multi-annual S2 crop dataset** (https://zenodo.org/records/19949051): CC-BY-4.0 and verified, but a single district. It is only useful as a small extra test region.

## Not recommended, and why

All seven records exist and their licences come from the registry records. "Today" means the registry record was read. "From memory" means I recalled the content details (classes, regions) and did not re-read them.

## Details

- **PASTIS** (https://zenodo.org/records/5012942):
  - It holds S2 time-series patches over French agricultural parcels with crop-type labels.
  - From memory, it has 18 classes.
  - It is the standard benchmark for parcel segmentation. You can derive per-parcel classification from it.
- **BreizhCrops** (https://zenodo.org/records/14585341):
  - It has per-parcel S2 time series (top-of-atmosphere and bottom-of-atmosphere) for Brittany.
  - The paper compares seven deep networks and a Random Forest baseline.
  - Loaders and pretrained weights are at github.com/dl4sits/breizhcrops, as named in the paper abstract.
  - It covers one region and one year.
  - Paper: doi 10.5194/isprs-archives-xliii-b2-2020-1545-2020.
- **EuroCropsML** (https://zenodo.org/records/15095445):
  - It has 706,683 labelled parcels over 176 crop classes in Latvia, Portugal and Estonia, with per-parcel median S2 L1C series for 2021.
  - It has the most classes of any option here.
  - Papers: doi 10.1038/s41597-025-04952-7 (dataset paper) and doi 10.1016/j.ophoto.2026.100117 (benchmark study).
- **S2-Agri** (https://zenodo.org/records/5815488; patch version https://zenodo.org/records/5815523):
  - It is parcel-based crop classification with S2 series.
  - The record text is brief. From memory it covers France and one season with about 20 classes, so read the related article first.
- **Sen4AgriNet** (https://huggingface.co/datasets/isp-uv-es/sen4agrinet-taco):
  - From memory, it is a very large multi-year dataset with about 168 declared-crop classes, covering Catalonia and France. Read the dataset card before downloading.
- **AgriSen-COG** (https://zenodo.org/records/7892012):
  - This record holds the rasterised LPIS ground-truth tiles.
  - I did not confirm whether the paired S2 imagery is in this record or a separate one.
- **CropHarvest** (DOI 10.5281/zenodo.10251170):
  - It has pre-extracted satellite time series, but most labels are crop versus non-crop. A smaller multi-class subset exists, from memory.
  - It is only a partial fit for your multi-class goal.

**Licences and your goal of publishing results and sharing models:**
- **CC-BY-4.0** (PASTIS, BreizhCrops, S2-Agri, AgriSen-COG): attribution only, so there are no problems.
- **CC-BY-SA-4.0** (EuroCropsML, Sen4AgriNet, CropHarvest): share-alike applies to the data and to datasets derived from it. Publishing results and models is normally fine. If you plan to release a derived dataset, it must carry the same licence. Check this with your supervisor.

## Known, not confirmed

- **TimeSen2Crop** (Austria, about 1M labelled S2 samples, 16 crop classes from memory):
  - The paper DOI resolves (10.1109/JSTARS.2021.3073965) and OpenAIRE lists CC-BY-4.0.
  - I did not find the data record itself, so check the paper's data link.
- **Peru, multi-annual S2 crop dataset** (https://zenodo.org/records/19949051): CC-BY-4.0 and verified, but a single district. It is only useful as a small extra test region.

## Not recommended, and why

- **ZueriCrop:** the Hugging Face copy is CC-BY-NC-4.0. I could not confirm the original terms, and the non-commercial clause may block sharing models.
- **IBM/NASA multi-temporal crop classification:** it uses Harmonized Landsat-Sentinel imagery with US CDL labels, not pure S2.
- **EuroCrops (labels only):** it has no imagery. EuroCropsML is the version with S2 attached.
- **National crop maps for Germany (Zenodo 6451848):** these are derived maps, access is by application, and no licence is stated.

## What I could not find

- TimeMatch, DENETHOR, Munich480, AI4Boundaries and SICKLE returned nothing usable from the registries, so I did not evaluate them.
- The Datacite and Zenodo searches failed intermittently, so the list may be incomplete.
- The literature lookup for PASTIS returned unrelated papers, so I list no paper for PASTIS, S2-Agri, Sen4AgriNet or CropHarvest.

## Next step

You can start today with **BreizhCrops** or **EuroCropsML**. Both are small, open, ready-made time series with baselines, and they suit a master's timeline. For a second benchmark with more spatial structure, add **PASTIS**.

The interactive page is at `/private/tmp/e-yrE7eT/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`.

✓ pass 01-recall-breizhcrops

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I'm answering from memory and haven't checked the live pages. Licences and hosting change, and some licences are stated differently on the paper, the repo and the data host. Check each licence on the data page before you rely on it.

## Best fits

| Dataset | Region / scale | What you get | Licence (as I recall) | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **PASTIS / PASTIS-R** | France, 2,433 patches of 128×128 px, about 124k parcels | S2 time series (10 bands, about 35–70 dates per patch in 2019). PASTIS-R adds S1. 18 crop classes plus background. Gives parcel instance masks, so it works for semantic or panoptic tasks. | CC-BY 4.0 | Zenodo ("PASTIS"), `VSainteuf/pastis-benchmark` on GitHub |
| **EuroCropsML** | Estonia, Latvia and Portugal, about 700k parcels | Parcel-level S2 time series already matched to EuroCrops labels, with a few-shot and transfer-learning setup. Many classes, from the HCAT taxonomy. | CC-BY 4.0 | Zenodo, `dida-do/eurocropsml` on GitHub |
| **BreizhCrops** | Brittany, France, about 600k parcels | Pixel-averaged S2 time series per field (L1C and L2A) with crop labels. A good fit for parcel-level time-series models like Transformers and LSTMs. | Check Zenodo and the repo | `dbuscombe-usgs/breizhcrops` successor at `dl4sits/BreizhCrops`, Zenodo |
| **ZueriCrop** | Canton of Zurich, Switzerland, about 116k parcels | S2 time series with a hierarchical crop taxonomy (48 classes). Used for instance segmentation. | Check the repo | `0zgur0/ms-convSTAR` on GitHub |
| **Sen4AgriNet** | Catalonia and France, 2019–2020 | S2 time series built from CAP declarations. Many classes in a hierarchy, with patch-level and parcel-level labels. | Check the repo | `Orion-AI-Lab/S4A` on GitHub |
| **CropHarvest** | Global, about 90k points | Labelled points, each paired with S2, S1, weather and elevation. It has a binary crop/non-crop label and a multiclass crop type label. The label coverage is uneven. | Labels come from many sources and are mostly CC-BY-SA 4.0 | Zenodo, `nasaharvest/cropharvest` on GitHub |

## If you want other regions or smallholder settings
- **AgriFieldNet (India)** has multi-temporal S2 with field-level labels. **Western Cape / South Africa crop type** and the Kenya, Ghana, Zambia and Tanzania sets are also relevant. Most were on Radiant MLHub, which has moved to Source Cooperative (source.coop), so search both. Several are CC-BY 4.0, but licences differ by dataset.
- **GEO-Bench** (`m-SA-crop-type`, `m-cashew-plant`) is a ready-made benchmark with standard splits and comparable baselines.
- **PANGAEA** bundles PASTIS and other crop sets behind one loader.

## Notes on licences
- **CC-BY 4.0** is the safest option. You can publish results and share trained models as long as you cite the dataset.
- **CC-BY-SA** (CropHarvest labels, and some EuroCrops-derived data) is more awkward. Whether a trained model counts as an "adaptation" is legally unsettled. Ask your supervisor or your university's data office before releasing weights trained on SA data.
- **Labels from official sources** (the CAP/LPIS parcel data behind EuroCrops, BreizhCrops and Sen4AgriNet) come from open-government data, but the dataset packagers set the licence you actually inherit. Read the dataset's own licence statement.
- **Sentinel-2 imagery** itself is free and open under Copernicus terms, so it doesn't restrict you.

## Recommendation
Start with **PASTIS-R**. It has a clean licence, strong published baselines (U-TAE, TSViT and others), and a patch format that works for both pixel-wise and parcel-wise classification. Add **EuroCropsML** or **BreizhCrops** if you need a larger parcel-level time-series set, or a second region to test generalisation.

I can browse the dataset pages and confirm the exact licence text for your shortlist if you'd like.

## Best fits

| Dataset | Region / scale | What you get | Licence (as I recall) | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **PASTIS / PASTIS-R** | France, 2,433 patches of 128×128 px, about 124k parcels | S2 time series (10 bands, about 35–70 dates per patch in 2019). PASTIS-R adds S1. 18 crop classes plus background. Gives parcel instance masks, so it works for semantic or panoptic tasks. | CC-BY 4.0 | Zenodo ("PASTIS"), `VSainteuf/pastis-benchmark` on GitHub |
| **EuroCropsML** | Estonia, Latvia and Portugal, about 700k parcels | Parcel-level S2 time series already matched to EuroCrops labels, with a few-shot and transfer-learning setup. Many classes, from the HCAT taxonomy. | CC-BY 4.0 | Zenodo, `dida-do/eurocropsml` on GitHub |
| **BreizhCrops** | Brittany, France, about 600k parcels | Pixel-averaged S2 time series per field (L1C and L2A) with crop labels. A good fit for parcel-level time-series models like Transformers and LSTMs. | Check Zenodo and the repo | `dbuscombe-usgs/breizhcrops` successor at `dl4sits/BreizhCrops`, Zenodo |
| **ZueriCrop** | Canton of Zurich, Switzerland, about 116k parcels | S2 time series with a hierarchical crop taxonomy (48 classes). Used for instance segmentation. | Check the repo | `0zgur0/ms-convSTAR` on GitHub |
| **Sen4AgriNet** | Catalonia and France, 2019–2020 | S2 time series built from CAP declarations. Many classes in a hierarchy, with patch-level and parcel-level labels. | Check the repo | `Orion-AI-Lab/S4A` on GitHub |
| **CropHarvest** | Global, about 90k points | Labelled points, each paired with S2, S1, weather and elevation. It has a binary crop/non-crop label and a multiclass crop type label. The label coverage is uneven. | Labels come from many sources and are mostly CC-BY-SA 4.0 | Zenodo, `nasaharvest/cropharvest` on GitHub |

- **AgriFieldNet (India)** has multi-temporal S2 with field-level labels. **Western Cape / South Africa crop type** and the Kenya, Ghana, Zambia and Tanzania sets are also relevant. - **GEO-Bench** (`m-SA-crop-type`, `m-cashew-plant`) is a ready-made benchmark with standard splits and comparable baselines.
- **PANGAEA** bundles PASTIS and other crop sets behind one loader.

- **Labels from official sources** (the CAP/LPIS parcel data behind EuroCrops, BreizhCrops and Sen4AgriNet) come from open-government data, but the dataset packagers set the licence you actually inherit. Read the dataset's own licence statement.
- **Sentinel-2 imagery** itself is free and open under Copernicus terms, so it doesn't restrict you.

## Recommendation
Start with **PASTIS-R**. It has a clean licence, strong published baselines (U-TAE, TSViT and others), and a patch format that works for both pixel-wise and parcel-wise classification. Add **EuroCropsML** or **BreizhCrops** if you need a larger parcel-level time-series set, or a second region to test generalisation.

I can browse the dataset pages and confirm the exact licence text for your shortlist if you'd like.

I'm answering from memory and haven't checked the live pages. Licences and hosting change, and some licences are stated differently on the paper, the repo and the data host. Check each licence on the data page before you rely on it.

## Best fits

| Dataset | Region / scale | What you get | Licence (as I recall) | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **PASTIS / PASTIS-R** | France, 2,433 patches of 128×128 px, about 124k parcels | S2 time series (10 bands, about 35–70 dates per patch in 2019). PASTIS-R adds S1. 18 crop classes plus background. Gives parcel instance masks, so it works for semantic or panoptic tasks. | CC-BY 4.0 | Zenodo ("PASTIS"), `VSainteuf/pastis-benchmark` on GitHub |
| **EuroCropsML** | Estonia, Latvia and Portugal, about 700k parcels | Parcel-level S2 time series already matched to EuroCrops labels, with a few-shot and transfer-learning setup. Many classes, from the HCAT taxonomy. | CC-BY 4.0 | Zenodo, `dida-do/eurocropsml` on GitHub |
| **BreizhCrops** | Brittany, France, about 600k parcels | Pixel-averaged S2 time series per field (L1C and L2A) with crop labels. A good fit for parcel-level time-series models like Transformers and LSTMs. | Check Zenodo and the repo | `dbuscombe-usgs/breizhcrops` successor at `dl4sits/BreizhCrops`, Zenodo |
| **ZueriCrop** | Canton of Zurich, Switzerland, about 116k parcels | S2 time series with a hierarchical crop taxonomy (48 classes). Used for instance segmentation. | Check the repo | `0zgur0/ms-convSTAR` on GitHub |
| **Sen4AgriNet** | Catalonia and France, 2019–2020 | S2 time series built from CAP declarations. Many classes in a hierarchy, with patch-level and parcel-level labels. | Check the repo | `Orion-AI-Lab/S4A` on GitHub |
| **CropHarvest** | Global, about 90k points | Labelled points, each paired with S2, S1, weather and elevation. It has a binary crop/non-crop label and a multiclass crop type label. The label coverage is uneven. | Labels come from many sources and are mostly CC-BY-SA 4.0 | Zenodo, `nasaharvest/cropharvest` on GitHub |

## If you want other regions or smallholder settings
- **AgriFieldNet (India)** has multi-temporal S2 with field-level labels. **Western Cape / South Africa crop type** and the Kenya, Ghana, Zambia and Tanzania sets are also relevant. Most were on Radiant MLHub, which has moved to Source Cooperative (source.coop), so search both. Several are CC-BY 4.0, but licences differ by dataset.
- **GEO-Bench** (`m-SA-crop-type`, `m-cashew-plant`) is a ready-made benchmark with standard splits and comparable baselines.
- **PANGAEA** bundles PASTIS and other crop sets behind one loader.

## Notes on licences
- **CC-BY 4.0** is the safest option. You can publish results and share trained models as long as you cite the dataset.
- **CC-BY-SA** (CropHarvest labels, and some EuroCrops-derived data) is more awkward. Whether a trained model counts as an "adaptation" is legally unsettled. Ask your supervisor or your university's data office before releasing weights trained on SA data.
- **Labels from official sources** (the CAP/LPIS parcel data behind EuroCrops, BreizhCrops and Sen4AgriNet) come from open-government data, but the dataset packagers set the licence you actually inherit. Read the dataset's own licence statement.
- **Sentinel-2 imagery** itself is free and open under Copernicus terms, so it doesn't restrict you.

## Recommendation
Start with **PASTIS-R**. It has a clean licence, strong published baselines (U-TAE, TSViT and others), and a patch format that works for both pixel-wise and parcel-wise classification. Add **EuroCropsML** or **BreizhCrops** if you need a larger parcel-level time-series set, or a second region to test generalisation.

## Best fits (crop-type labels already paired with Sentinel-2 time series)

| Dataset | Region / labels | Unit | Licence | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **PASTIS / PASTIS-R** | France, 18 crop classes plus background, about 2,400 patches of 128×128 px, 30–60 S2 dates each. PASTIS-R adds Sentinel-1. | Parcel-level panoptic and semantic labels | CC BY 4.0 | Zenodo (search "PASTIS"); code and baselines (U-TAE) at `github.com/VSainteuf/pastis-benchmark` |
| **BreizhCrops** | Brittany, France, about 600k field parcels. S2 L1C and L2A time series, with 9 main crop classes in the standard setup. | Field-averaged time series (good for LSTM, transformer or TempCNN) | Verify. Labels come from the French LPIS, which is open (Etalab licence), and the code is MIT. | `github.com/dl4sits/BreizhCrops`, data on Zenodo, and a PyTorch loader that downloads it for you |
| **EuroCropsML** | Estonia, Latvia and Portugal, many crop classes from the EuroCrops HCAT taxonomy, with S2 time series ready to use | Parcel-level | Likely CC BY 4.0 (verify) | Zenodo, with a Python package |
| **ZueriCrop** | Zurich and Thurgau, Switzerland, about 48 crop classes, about 116k parcels, S2 time series | Parcel-level instance and semantic labels | Verify. Not all datasets in this area are CC BY. | `github.com/0zgur0/ms-convSTAR` (links to the data) |
| **Sen4AgriNet** | Catalonia and France, 2019–2020, hierarchical crop labels from CAP declarations, S2 time series | Parcel-level | Verify. I believe it is open. | GitHub and Zenodo (search "Sen4AgriNet") |

## Related, but less direct
- **CropHarvest** is global and multi-source, but mostly binary crop vs. non-crop. Only a subset has crop-type labels. It is CC BY-SA 4.0, which is share-alike. Check how that affects the models and data you release.
- **EuroCrops** has harmonised LPIS labels across the EU but no imagery. You would have to build the S2 pairing yourself, which is exactly what you want to avoid.
- **TimeMatch** is useful if you want to study domain shift across regions and years.
- **African crop-type datasets** (Kenya, Ghana, Tanzania, South Africa) have moved from Radiant MLHub to **Source Cooperative**. They are typically CC BY 4.0, but each one differs.

## Recommendation
1. **Start with PASTIS-R.** It is the most widely used benchmark, so your results are directly comparable with published numbers. It is CC BY 4.0, which allows publishing results and sharing trained models with attribution. Baselines and loaders are included.
2. **Add BreizhCrops or EuroCropsML** if you want a pixel- or parcel-level time-series setup, or a second region to test generalisation. Both are larger and simpler to load than PASTIS.

## Licence caveats
- **Attribution (CC BY):** you can publish results and release model weights. Cite the dataset and keep the credit.
- **Share-alike (CC BY-SA):** this applies to adapted datasets. Whether it extends to trained model weights is legally unsettled. If you want a clean release, prefer CC BY datasets.
- **Underlying label sources:** the LPIS and CAP data come from national agencies, and their terms are separate from the dataset's licence. Read the "licence" section of each Zenodo record.
- **Sentinel-2 imagery:** this is free and open under the Copernicus terms.

I can check the exact licences and download links on the live pages if you'd like.

Labels come from the French LPIS, which is open (Etalab licence), and the code is MIT. | `github.com/dl4sits/BreizhCrops`, data on Zenodo, and a PyTorch loader that downloads it for you |
| **EuroCropsML** | Estonia, Latvia and Portugal, many crop classes from the EuroCrops HCAT taxonomy, with S2 time series ready to use | Parcel-level | Likely CC BY 4.0 (verify) | Zenodo, with a Python package |
| **ZueriCrop** | Zurich and Thurgau, Switzerland, about 48 crop classes, about 116k parcels, S2 time series | Parcel-level instance and semantic labels | Verify. Not all datasets in this area are CC BY. | `github.com/0zgur0/ms-convSTAR` (links to the data) |
| **Sen4AgriNet** | Catalonia and France, 2019–2020, hierarchical crop labels from CAP declarations, S2 time series | Parcel-level | Verify. I believe it is open. | GitHub and Zenodo (search "Sen4AgriNet") |

## Related, but less direct
- **CropHarvest** is global and multi-source, but mostly binary crop vs. non-crop. Only a subset has crop-type labels. It is CC BY-SA 4.0, which is share-alike. Check how that affects the models and data you release.
- **EuroCrops** has harmonised LPIS labels across the EU but no imagery. You would have to build the S2 pairing yourself, which is exactly what you want to avoid.
- **TimeMatch** is useful if you want to study domain shift across regions and years.
- **African crop-type datasets** (Kenya, Ghana, Tanzania, South Africa) have moved from Radiant MLHub to **Source Cooperative**. They are typically CC BY 4.0, but each one differs.

## Recommendation
1. **Start with PASTIS-R.** It is the most widely used benchmark, so your results are directly comparable with published numbers. It is CC BY 4.0, which allows publishing results and sharing trained models with attribution. Baselines and loaders are included.
2. **Add BreizhCrops or EuroCropsML** if you want a pixel- or parcel-level time-series setup, or a second region to test generalisation. Both are larger and simpler to load than PASTIS.

## Licence caveats
- **Attribution (CC BY):** you can publish results and release model weights. Cite the dataset and keep the credit.
- **Share-alike (CC BY-SA):** this applies to adapted datasets. Whether it extends to trained model weights is legally unsettled. If you want a clean release, prefer CC BY datasets.
- **Underlying label sources:** the LPIS and CAP data come from national agencies, and their terms are separate from the dataset's licence. Read the "licence" section of each Zenodo record.
- **Sentinel-2 imagery:** this is free and open under the Copernicus terms.

I can check the exact licences and download links on the live pages if you'd like.

Prompt

I'm doing a master's project on crop type classification from Sentinel-2 image time series. I need ground-truth labels for actual crops (wheat, maize, rapeseed and so on, not just cropland versus non-cropland) for individual fields or pixels, already paired with multi-temporal Sentinel-2 data, because I don't have time to build my own download and preprocessing pipeline. The data also needs a licence that lets me publish results and share trained models. Which benchmark datasets fit, and where do I get them?

Graders — what "good" means for this case
00-skill-firedtool_used
tool"Skill"
input_match"\"skill\"\\s*:\\s*\"(?:[\\w-]+:)?dataset-scout\""
01-recall-breizhcropsllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the BreizhCrops dataset (also written Breizhcrops: Sentinel-2 time series of agricultural parcels in Brittany, France, with crop type labels, by Russwurm and colleagues) as a candidate crop type classification benchmark. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

02-recall-pastisllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the PASTIS dataset (Panoptic Agricultural Satellite TIme Series, by Sainte Fare Garnot and Landrieu; any variant such as PASTIS-R) as a candidate crop type benchmark built on Sentinel-2 time series. FAIL if the answer does not mention PASTIS, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

03-recall-sen4agrinetllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the Sen4AgriNet dataset (also known as S4A, the Sentinel-2 multi-year, multi-country crop classification benchmark covering Catalonia and France, by Sykas and colleagues) as a candidate crop type benchmark. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

04-recall-timesen2cropllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the TimeSen2Crop dataset (about one million labelled Sentinel-2 time-series samples for 16 crop types in Austria, by Weikmann, Paris and Bruzzone) as a candidate crop type benchmark. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

05-access-pastis-zenodo-cc-byllm

PASS if the final answer states that PASTIS (Panoptic Agricultural Satellite TIme Series) can be downloaded openly, without an application, and that its licence is Creative Commons Attribution (CC BY, version 4.0). Saying that it is hosted on Zenodo is welcome but not required. FAIL if the answer does not mention PASTIS, gives no licence for it, gives a different licence (for example a non-commercial or share-alike one), or says access must be requested.

06-access-sen4agrinet-mitllm

PASS if the final answer states that Sen4AgriNet (S4A) can be downloaded openly, without an application (for example from the Hugging Face Hub, Dropbox or Google Drive), and that it is released under the MIT licence. FAIL if the answer does not mention Sen4AgriNet, gives no licence for it, gives a different licence, or says access must be requested.

07-access-timesen2crop-zenodo-cc-byllm

PASS if the final answer states that TimeSen2Crop can be downloaded openly, without an application, and that its licence is Creative Commons Attribution (CC BY, version 4.0). Saying that it is hosted on Zenodo is welcome but not required. FAIL if the answer does not mention TimeSen2Crop, gives no licence for it, gives a different licence, or says access must be requested.

08-trap-eurosat-no-crop-typesllm

PASS if the final answer does not present EuroSAT (the Sentinel-2 land use and land cover patch dataset of 27,000 images in 10 classes, by Helber and colleagues) as providing crop type labels or multi-temporal Sentinel-2 time series. Not mentioning EuroSAT passes. Mentioning it while saying that it has only single-date patches and only broad classes (annual crop, permanent crop, pasture) rather than crop species passes. FAIL if the answer recommends or lists EuroSAT as a crop type classification dataset for this project without stating that limitation.

09-long-tailllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists at least one of these less widely known crop type datasets that pair labels with Sentinel-2 time series: EuroCropsML (the ready-to-use machine learning version of EuroCrops for Latvia, Portugal and Estonia, by Reuss and colleagues); or the South Africa Crop Type Competition dataset (also called the Western Cape crop type dataset or the Radiant Earth "Spot the Crop" challenge data, on Radiant MLHub or Source Cooperative). FAIL if the answer mentions neither of them.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Several good household-level datasets exist, and four of them you can download today with no application. I checked these on 2026-11-12. Some registry lookups timed out, so I mark which facts are confirmed and which are from memory.

## What I assumed you need
- Per-household meters, not feeder totals (must-have).
- Hourly or finer resolution (must-have).
- At least a year of history per home (must-have).
- Hundreds of homes or more (must-have).
- Research use. I did not assume commercial use.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Households | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | GoiEner smart meters (Spain) | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | ~25k (v7.1 lists 28,900 profiles) | Strong | Today |
| 2 | London Smart Meters, Low Carbon London (UK) | Open download, minutes | CC-BY (from memory) | 5,567 | Strong | Page confirmed today; licence from memory |
| 3 | CER Irish Smart Metering trial | Signed request form to ISSDA, days | ISSDA data-use terms | ~5,000 homes and businesses | Strong | Page and process confirmed today |
| 4 | Smart-Grid Smart-City trial (Australia) | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | Thousands (from memory) | Strong | Today |
| 5 | Pecan Street Dataport (US) | University account, ~3 business days | University licence | Hundreds or more (from memory) | Strong | Today |
| 6 | HEAPO (Switzerland) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | 1,408 | Partial | Today |
| 7 | iFlex (Norway) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | Several hundred (from memory) | Partial | Today |
| 8 | Ausgrid Solar Homes (Australia) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | 300 | Partial | Record registered today; contents from memory |

## Details
- **GoiEner:** Hourly kWh per customer, with processed files kept to series longer than a year. Download from Zenodo with no account, and prefer [v7.1](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22313409) over the [2022 release](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.7362094). Customers are mostly in northern Spain and some have PV or are businesses, so check the metadata file.
- **London:** Half-hourly data, Nov 2011 to Feb 2014, from the [London Datastore](https://data.london.gov.uk/dataset/smartmeter-energy-consumption-data-in-london-households-vqm0d) (about 10 GB). Cleaned copies are on [Zenodo](https://zenodo.org/records/4656091). Start dates are staggered, so most homes have 1–2 years.
- **CER Ireland:** The [ISSDA record](https://doi.org/10.7929/ISSDA/BX59EU) has 30-minute data over about 18 months (from memory). To get it, sign the research request form and email it to issda@ucd.ie. Each student needs their own form, and the data includes businesses.
- **Smart-Grid Smart-City:** [data.gov.au](https://data.gov.au/data/dataset/smart-grid-smart-city-customer-trial-data) has half-hourly readings linked to demographics and tariffs, licensed CC BY 4.0. The trial ran 2010–14.
- **Pecan Street:** [Dataport](https://dataport.pecanstreet.org) requires a university email and proof of affiliation. The page says approval takes about 3 business days. Commercial use needs a paid licence. I could not see the free tier's coverage.
- **HEAPO and iFlex:** [HEAPO](https://zenodo.org/records/15056919) covers heat-pump homes only, so loads are atypical. [iFlex](https://zenodo.org/records/8248802) is hourly but comes from a pricing experiment lasting about a year, with treatment effects.
- **Ausgrid:** [This figshare record](https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.32975426.v1) is net demand for homes with rooftop PV, so gross load is hidden.

## Known, not confirmed
- Exact time spans and household counts for GoiEner, SGSC and Pecan Street are from memory.
- Ausgrid's original 300-home, 3-year release is also from memory.

## Not recommended
- **UCI ElectricityLoadDiagrams:** 370 clients, mostly commercial (from memory).
- **REFIT and UK-DALE:** Only 20 and 5 homes (from memory).

## What I could not find
I did not check a few other possible sources: Low Carbon London extras, Dutch or Nordic utility releases, and academic forecasting benchmarks. I also did not find papers that used these datasets, because I ran out of budget.

## Next step
Start with GoiEner and London today. They need no approval and together give you tens of thousands of homes. In parallel, send the CER form, since it is the classic benchmark for comparing against published results.

Companion page: `/private/tmp/e-JK1LHZ/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Households | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | GoiEner smart meters (Spain) | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | ~25k (v7.1 lists 28,900 profiles) | Strong | Today |
| 2 | London Smart Meters, Low Carbon London (UK) | Open download, minutes | CC-BY (from memory) | 5,567 | Strong | Page confirmed today; licence from memory |
| 3 | CER Irish Smart Metering trial | Signed request form to ISSDA, days | ISSDA data-use terms | ~5,000 homes and businesses | Strong | Page and process confirmed today |
| 4 | Smart-Grid Smart-City trial (Australia) | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | Thousands (from memory) | Strong | Today |
| 5 | Pecan Street Dataport (US) | University account, ~3 business days | University licence | Hundreds or more (from memory) | Strong | Today |
| 6 | HEAPO (Switzerland) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | 1,408 | Partial | Today |
| 7 | iFlex (Norway) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | Several hundred (from memory) | Partial | Today |
| 8 | Ausgrid Solar Homes (Australia) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | 300 | Partial | Record registered today; contents from memory |

## Details
- **GoiEner:** Hourly kWh per customer, with processed files kept to series longer than a year. Download from Zenodo with no account, and prefer [v7.1](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22313409) over the [2022 release](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.7362094). Customers are mostly in northern Spain and some have PV or are businesses, so check the metadata file.
- **London:** Half-hourly data, Nov 2011 to Feb 2014, from the [London Datastore](https://data.london.gov.uk/dataset/smartmeter-energy-consumption-data-in-london-households-vqm0d) (about 10 GB). Cleaned copies are on [Zenodo](https://zenodo.org/records/4656091). Start dates are staggered, so most homes have 1–2 years.
- **CER Ireland:** The [ISSDA record](https://doi.org/10.7929/ISSDA/BX59EU) has 30-minute data over about 18 months (from memory). To get it, sign the research request form and email it to issda@ucd.ie. Each student needs their own form, and the data includes businesses.
- **Smart-Grid Smart-City:** [data.gov.au](https://data.gov.au/data/dataset/smart-grid-smart-city-customer-trial-data) has half-hourly readings linked to demographics and tariffs, licensed CC BY 4.0. The trial ran 2010–14.
- **Pecan Street:** [Dataport](https://dataport.pecanstreet.org) requires a university email and proof of affiliation. The page says approval takes about 3 business days. Commercial use needs a paid licence. I could not see the free tier's coverage.
- **HEAPO and iFlex:** [HEAPO](https://zenodo.org/records/15056919) covers heat-pump homes only, so loads are atypical. [iFlex](https://zenodo.org/records/8248802) is hourly but comes from a pricing experiment lasting about a year, with treatment effects.
- **Ausgrid:** [This figshare record](https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.32975426.v1) is net demand for homes with rooftop PV, so gross load is hidden.

## Known, not confirmed
- Exact time spans and household counts for GoiEner, SGSC and Pecan Street are from memory.
- Ausgrid's original 300-home, 3-year release is also from memory.

## Not recommended
- **UCI ElectricityLoadDiagrams:** 370 clients, mostly commercial (from memory).
- **REFIT and UK-DALE:** Only 20 and 5 homes (from memory).

## What I could not find
I did not check a few other possible sources: Low Carbon London extras, Dutch or Nordic utility releases, and academic forecasting benchmarks. I also did not find papers that used these datasets, because I ran out of budget.

I did not check a few other possible sources: Low Carbon London extras, Dutch or Nordic utility releases, and academic forecasting benchmarks. I also did not find papers that used these datasets, because I ran out of budget.

## Next step
Start with GoiEner and London today. They need no approval and together give you tens of thousands of homes. In parallel, send the CER form, since it is the classic benchmark for comparing against published results.

✓ pass 10-long-tail

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Several good household-level datasets exist, and four of them you can download today with no application. I checked these on 2026-11-12. Some registry lookups timed out, so I mark which facts are confirmed and which are from memory.

## What I assumed you need
- Per-household meters, not feeder totals (must-have).
- Hourly or finer resolution (must-have).
- At least a year of history per home (must-have).
- Hundreds of homes or more (must-have).
- Research use. I did not assume commercial use.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Households | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | GoiEner smart meters (Spain) | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | ~25k (v7.1 lists 28,900 profiles) | Strong | Today |
| 2 | London Smart Meters, Low Carbon London (UK) | Open download, minutes | CC-BY (from memory) | 5,567 | Strong | Page confirmed today; licence from memory |
| 3 | CER Irish Smart Metering trial | Signed request form to ISSDA, days | ISSDA data-use terms | ~5,000 homes and businesses | Strong | Page and process confirmed today |
| 4 | Smart-Grid Smart-City trial (Australia) | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | Thousands (from memory) | Strong | Today |
| 5 | Pecan Street Dataport (US) | University account, ~3 business days | University licence | Hundreds or more (from memory) | Strong | Today |
| 6 | HEAPO (Switzerland) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | 1,408 | Partial | Today |
| 7 | iFlex (Norway) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | Several hundred (from memory) | Partial | Today |
| 8 | Ausgrid Solar Homes (Australia) | Open download | CC-BY-4.0 | 300 | Partial | Record registered today; contents from memory |

## Details
- **GoiEner:** Hourly kWh per customer, with processed files kept to series longer than a year. Download from Zenodo with no account, and prefer [v7.1](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22313409) over the [2022 release](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.7362094). Customers are mostly in northern Spain and some have PV or are businesses, so check the metadata file.
- **London:** Half-hourly data, Nov 2011 to Feb 2014, from the [London Datastore](https://data.london.gov.uk/dataset/smartmeter-energy-consumption-data-in-london-households-vqm0d) (about 10 GB). Cleaned copies are on [Zenodo](https://zenodo.org/records/4656091). Start dates are staggered, so most homes have 1–2 years.
- **CER Ireland:** The [ISSDA record](https://doi.org/10.7929/ISSDA/BX59EU) has 30-minute data over about 18 months (from memory). To get it, sign the research request form and email it to issda@ucd.ie. Each student needs their own form, and the data includes businesses.
- **Smart-Grid Smart-City:** [data.gov.au](https://data.gov.au/data/dataset/smart-grid-smart-city-customer-trial-data) has half-hourly readings linked to demographics and tariffs, licensed CC BY 4.0. The trial ran 2010–14.
- **Pecan Street:** [Dataport](https://dataport.pecanstreet.org) requires a university email and proof of affiliation. The page says approval takes about 3 business days. Commercial use needs a paid licence. I could not see the free tier's coverage.
- **HEAPO and iFlex:** [HEAPO](https://zenodo.org/records/15056919) covers heat-pump homes only, so loads are atypical. [iFlex](https://zenodo.org/records/8248802) is hourly but comes from a pricing experiment lasting about a year, with treatment effects.
- **Ausgrid:** [This figshare record](https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.32975426.v1) is net demand for homes with rooftop PV, so gross load is hidden.

## Known, not confirmed
- Exact time spans and household counts for GoiEner, SGSC and Pecan Street are from memory.
- Ausgrid's original 300-home, 3-year release is also from memory.

## Not recommended
- **UCI ElectricityLoadDiagrams:** 370 clients, mostly commercial (from memory).
- **REFIT and UK-DALE:** Only 20 and 5 homes (from memory).

## What I could not find
I did not check a few other possible sources: Low Carbon London extras, Dutch or Nordic utility releases, and academic forecasting benchmarks. I also did not find papers that used these datasets, because I ran out of budget.

## Next step
Start with GoiEner and London today. They need no approval and together give you tens of thousands of homes. In parallel, send the CER form, since it is the classic benchmark for comparing against published results.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Several good datasets are free to download today, and one big one needs a short application. I found nothing at the scale and quality you want that is locked behind a long approval process.

I checked on 2026-11-12. Registry records and Zenodo pages are marked "confirmed". Access and licence details I recalled but couldn't read are marked "from memory". DataCite search and parts of the Zenodo and Hugging Face searches kept failing, so the list may be incomplete.

## What I assumed you need
- **R1:** consumption per household, not feeder or grid totals.
- **R2:** hundreds of households or more.
- **R3:** at least a year of history per household.
- **R4:** hourly or finer.
- Public data you can start on now. I'd count a short application as acceptable.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | GoiEner Smart Meter Dataset v7.1 (Spain) | Direct download from Zenodo | CC BY 4.0 | 28,900 customer series, hourly, about 9 GB | Strong | Today |
| 2 | Residential load profiles, Switzerland | Direct download from Zenodo | CC BY 4.0 | 2,447 installations, 15-minute, 2023–2024 | Strong | Today |
| 3 | London Households (UKPN Low Carbon London) | Download from London Datastore | Open Government Licence | 5,567 households, half-hourly, Nov 2011–Feb 2014 | Strong | Page exists, access and licence from memory |
| 4 | CER Irish Smart Metering Trial | Application plus Data Use Agreement through ISSDA, weeks | Custom data use terms | About 5,000 homes and small businesses, half-hourly, about 1.5 years | Strong | Application form and agreement confirmed today. Size and span from memory |
| 5 | CEEW India smart meter data | Direct download from Dataverse | CC0 | About 1,000 households, 3-minute, 2019–2021 | Partial | Today |
| 6 | IDEAL (Edinburgh, UK) | Download from DataShare | CC BY 4.0 (from memory) | 255 homes, about 23 months, plus appliance data | Partial | Page exists, details from memory |
| 7 | Smart-Grid Smart-City trial (Australia) | Download from data.gov.au | CC BY (from memory) | Tens of thousands of customers, half-hourly, 2010–2014 | Partial | Page exists, details from memory |
| 8 | Chinese households smart meter dataset | Direct download from figshare | CC BY 4.0 | 2,162 households, 24 or 96 readings per day | Partial | Today |

## Details
- **GoiEner** (https://zenodo.org/records/22313409)
  - **What it is:** a Basque and Navarre energy cooperative's customers, with an annual hourly matrix and reduced public metadata.
  - **Missing:** I couldn't confirm how long each customer's history is. Some customers have PV, and some may be businesses.
  - **Version:** use v7.1, not the 2022 record.
- **Switzerland** (https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21398769)
  - **What it is:** apartments, single-family houses, heat pumps and common building areas, so not all are whole households. It is recent and includes PV and heat-pump effects, which makes forecasting harder.
- **London**
  - **Missing:** many households joined partway through, so only some have a full year.
  - **Copies:** Kaggle has a copy, but use the London Datastore.
  - **Access:** the Datastore page timed out when I read it, so access and licence are from memory.
  - **Related:** a 2013 time-of-use pricing trial (1,100 households) is held separately at the UK Data Service. Its access tier is unknown, so check it before relying on it.
- **CER Ireland**
  - **Access:** the page shows "Request Access" and a custom Data Use Agreement. Approval time is from memory (days to a couple of weeks).
  - **Missing:** the trial applied tariff and stimulus changes to some homes, which affects the baseline.
- **CEEW India**
  - **Missing:** there are many supply outages, so the series have gaps and zeros. It also has a different demand pattern from European homes.
  - **Access:** the landing page was blocked for the checker. The registry says open under CC0.
- **IDEAL, Smart-Grid Smart-City and the Chinese dataset:** these are smaller or less certain. The Chinese set reports net power, so PV export masks demand for its 620 prosumers. I couldn't confirm its time span.

## Known, not confirmed
- **Ausgrid Solar Home data** (from memory: 300 homes, 30-minute, 3 years, open). The address I tried returned 404, so find the current link on Ausgrid's site.
- **SERL Observatory** (UK, 13,000+ households, half-hourly). A paper I found describes it, but I didn't check its access. From memory it is safeguarded data from the UK Data Service, so expect registration or an application.
- **Pecan Street Dataport** (US). The site wouldn't load. From memory, it is free for university researchers after a signed agreement, and the free tier has far fewer homes.
- **Hugging Face "OpenSynth/TUDelft-Electricity-Consumption"** bundles several household sets, including London and Dutch Liander data. It is handy for quick tests, but I didn't confirm its contents or span.

## Not recommended, and why
- **UCI ElectricityLoadDiagrams** and its Hugging Face copies. From memory it has 370 Portuguese clients, many commercial. I couldn't load the page to confirm.
- **UCI household power consumption.** From memory it covers one household.
- **"AI-Ready" Zenodo versions of the London data.** The demand-forecasting one is an hourly total across households, so it fails R1. The anomaly-detection one is a feature-engineered copy, so use the original.

## What I could not find
Nothing public that is both open and large with long multi-year hourly history across many homes. GoiEner is the closest. I didn't confirm the per-customer history length there, so check it first.

## Next step
Download GoiEner and the Swiss set today. Apply to ISSDA for the Irish data in parallel, because it is the best-known forecasting benchmark. London is a good fallback for a comparison dataset.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Several good datasets are free to download today, and one big one needs a short application. I found nothing at the scale and quality you want that is locked behind a long approval process.

I checked on 2026-11-12. Registry records and Zenodo pages are marked "confirmed". Access and licence details I recalled but couldn't read are marked "from memory". DataCite search and parts of the Zenodo and Hugging Face searches kept failing, so the list may be incomplete.

## What I assumed you need
- **R1:** consumption per household, not feeder or grid totals.
- **R2:** hundreds of households or more.
- **R3:** at least a year of history per household.
- **R4:** hourly or finer.
- Public data you can start on now. I'd count a short application as acceptable.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | GoiEner Smart Meter Dataset v7.1 (Spain) | Direct download from Zenodo | CC BY 4.0 | 28,900 customer series, hourly, about 9 GB | Strong | Today |
| 2 | Residential load profiles, Switzerland | Direct download from Zenodo | CC BY 4.0 | 2,447 installations, 15-minute, 2023–2024 | Strong | Today |
| 3 | London Households (UKPN Low Carbon London) | Download from London Datastore | Open Government Licence | 5,567 households, half-hourly, Nov 2011–Feb 2014 | Strong | Page exists, access and licence from memory |
| 4 | CER Irish Smart Metering Trial | Application plus Data Use Agreement through ISSDA, weeks | Custom data use terms | About 5,000 homes and small businesses, half-hourly, about 1.5 years | Strong | Application form and agreement confirmed today. Size and span from memory |
| 5 | CEEW India smart meter data | Direct download from Dataverse | CC0 | About 1,000 households, 3-minute, 2019–2021 | Partial | Today |
| 6 | IDEAL (Edinburgh, UK) | Download from DataShare | CC BY 4.0 (from memory) | 255 homes, about 23 months, plus appliance data | Partial | Page exists, details from memory |
| 7 | Smart-Grid Smart-City trial (Australia) | Download from data.gov.au | CC BY (from memory) | Tens of thousands of customers, half-hourly, 2010–2014 | Partial | Page exists, details from memory |
| 8 | Chinese households smart meter dataset | Direct download from figshare | CC BY 4.0 | 2,162 households, 24 or 96 readings per day | Partial | Today |

## Details
- **GoiEner** (https://zenodo.org/records/22313409)
  - **What it is:** a Basque and Navarre energy cooperative's customers, with an annual hourly matrix and reduced public metadata.
  - **Missing:** I couldn't confirm how long each customer's history is. Some customers have PV, and some may be businesses.
  - **Version:** use v7.1, not the 2022 record.
- **Switzerland** (https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21398769)
  - **What it is:** apartments, single-family houses, heat pumps and common building areas, so not all are whole households. It is recent and includes PV and heat-pump effects, which makes forecasting harder.
- **London**
  - **Missing:** many households joined partway through, so only some have a full year.
  - **Copies:** Kaggle has a copy, but use the London Datastore.
  - **Access:** the Datastore page timed out when I read it, so access and licence are from memory.
  - **Related:** a 2013 time-of-use pricing trial (1,100 households) is held separately at the UK Data Service. Its access tier is unknown, so check it before relying on it.
- **CER Ireland**
  - **Access:** the page shows "Request Access" and a custom Data Use Agreement. Approval time is from memory (days to a couple of weeks).
  - **Missing:** the trial applied tariff and stimulus changes to some homes, which affects the baseline.
- **CEEW India**
  - **Missing:** there are many supply outages, so the series have gaps and zeros. It also has a different demand pattern from European homes.
  - **Access:** the landing page was blocked for the checker. The registry says open under CC0.
- **IDEAL, Smart-Grid Smart-City and the Chinese dataset:** these are smaller or less certain. The Chinese set reports net power, so PV export masks demand for its 620 prosumers. I couldn't confirm its time span.

## Known, not confirmed
- **Ausgrid Solar Home data** (from memory: 300 homes, 30-minute, 3 years, open). The address I tried returned 404, so find the current link on Ausgrid's site.
- **SERL Observatory** (UK, 13,000+ households, half-hourly). A paper I found describes it, but I didn't check its access. From memory it is safeguarded data from the UK Data Service, so expect registration or an application.
- **Pecan Street Dataport** (US). The site wouldn't load. From memory, it is free for university researchers after a signed agreement, and the free tier has far fewer homes.
- **Hugging Face "OpenSynth/TUDelft-Electricity-Consumption"** bundles several household sets, including London and Dutch Liander data. It is handy for quick tests, but I didn't confirm its contents or span.

## Not recommended, and why
- **UCI ElectricityLoadDiagrams** and its Hugging Face copies. From memory it has 370 Portuguese clients, many commercial. I couldn't load the page to confirm.
- **UCI household power consumption.** From memory it covers one household.
- **"AI-Ready" Zenodo versions of the London data.** The demand-forecasting one is an hourly total across households, so it fails R1. The anomaly-detection one is a feature-engineered copy, so use the original.

## What I could not find
Nothing public that is both open and large with long multi-year hourly history across many homes. GoiEner is the closest. I didn't confirm the per-customer history length there, so check it first.

## Next step
Download GoiEner and the Swiss set today. Apply to ISSDA for the Irish data in parallel, because it is the best-known forecasting benchmark. London is a good fallback for a comparison dataset.

Interactive page: `/private/tmp/e-2Ua2Ic/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Several good datasets are free to download today, and one big one needs a short application. I found nothing at the scale and quality you want that is locked behind a long approval process.

I checked on 2026-11-12. Registry records and Zenodo pages are marked "confirmed". Access and licence details I recalled but couldn't read are marked "from memory". DataCite search and parts of the Zenodo and Hugging Face searches kept failing, so the list may be incomplete.

## What I assumed you need
- **R1:** consumption per household, not feeder or grid totals.
- **R2:** hundreds of households or more.
- **R3:** at least a year of history per household.
- **R4:** hourly or finer.
- Public data you can start on now. I'd count a short application as acceptable.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | GoiEner Smart Meter Dataset v7.1 (Spain) | Direct download from Zenodo | CC BY 4.0 | 28,900 customer series, hourly, about 9 GB | Strong | Today |
| 2 | Residential load profiles, Switzerland | Direct download from Zenodo | CC BY 4.0 | 2,447 installations, 15-minute, 2023–2024 | Strong | Today |
| 3 | London Households (UKPN Low Carbon London) | Download from London Datastore | Open Government Licence | 5,567 households, half-hourly, Nov 2011–Feb 2014 | Strong | Page exists, access and licence from memory |
| 4 | CER Irish Smart Metering Trial | Application plus Data Use Agreement through ISSDA, weeks | Custom data use terms | About 5,000 homes and small businesses, half-hourly, about 1.5 years | Strong | Application form and agreement confirmed today. Size and span from memory |
| 5 | CEEW India smart meter data | Direct download from Dataverse | CC0 | About 1,000 households, 3-minute, 2019–2021 | Partial | Today |
| 6 | IDEAL (Edinburgh, UK) | Download from DataShare | CC BY 4.0 (from memory) | 255 homes, about 23 months, plus appliance data | Partial | Page exists, details from memory |
| 7 | Smart-Grid Smart-City trial (Australia) | Download from data.gov.au | CC BY (from memory) | Tens of thousands of customers, half-hourly, 2010–2014 | Partial | Page exists, details from memory |
| 8 | Chinese households smart meter dataset | Direct download from figshare | CC BY 4.0 | 2,162 households, 24 or 96 readings per day | Partial | Today |

## Details
- **GoiEner** (https://zenodo.org/records/22313409)
  - **What it is:** a Basque and Navarre energy cooperative's customers, with an annual hourly matrix and reduced public metadata.
  - **Missing:** I couldn't confirm how long each customer's history is. Some customers have PV, and some may be businesses.
  - **Version:** use v7.1, not the 2022 record.
- **Switzerland** (https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21398769)
  - **What it is:** apartments, single-family houses, heat pumps and common building areas, so not all are whole households. It is recent and includes PV and heat-pump effects, which makes forecasting harder.
- **London**
  - **Missing:** many households joined partway through, so only some have a full year.
  - **Copies:** Kaggle has a copy, but use the London Datastore.
  - **Access:** the Datastore page timed out when I read it, so access and licence are from memory.
  - **Related:** a 2013 time-of-use pricing trial (1,100 households) is held separately at the UK Data Service. Its access tier is unknown, so check it before relying on it.
- **CER Ireland**
  - **Access:** the page shows "Request Access" and a custom Data Use Agreement. Approval time is from memory (days to a couple of weeks).
  - **Missing:** the trial applied tariff and stimulus changes to some homes, which affects the baseline.
- **CEEW India**
  - **Missing:** there are many supply outages, so the series have gaps and zeros. It also has a different demand pattern from European homes.
  - **Access:** the landing page was blocked for the checker. The registry says open under CC0.
- **IDEAL, Smart-Grid Smart-City and the Chinese dataset:** these are smaller or less certain. The Chinese set reports net power, so PV export masks demand for its 620 prosumers. I couldn't confirm its time span.

## Known, not confirmed
- **Ausgrid Solar Home data** (from memory: 300 homes, 30-minute, 3 years, open). The address I tried returned 404, so find the current link on Ausgrid's site.
- **SERL Observatory** (UK, 13,000+ households, half-hourly). A paper I found describes it, but I didn't check its access. From memory it is safeguarded data from the UK Data Service, so expect registration or an application.
- **Pecan Street Dataport** (US). The site wouldn't load. From memory, it is free for university researchers after a signed agreement, and the free tier has far fewer homes.
- **Hugging Face "OpenSynth/TUDelft-Electricity-Consumption"** bundles several household sets, including London and Dutch Liander data. It is handy for quick tests, but I didn't confirm its contents or span.

## Not recommended, and why
- **UCI ElectricityLoadDiagrams** and its Hugging Face copies. From memory it has 370 Portuguese clients, many commercial. I couldn't load the page to confirm.
- **UCI household power consumption.** From memory it covers one household.
- **"AI-Ready" Zenodo versions of the London data.** The demand-forecasting one is an hourly total across households, so it fails R1. The anomaly-detection one is a feature-engineered copy, so use the original.

## What I could not find
Nothing public that is both open and large with long multi-year hourly history across many homes. GoiEner is the closest. I didn't confirm the per-customer history length there, so check it first.

## Next step
Download GoiEner and the Swiss set today. Apply to ISSDA for the Irish data in parallel, because it is the best-known forecasting benchmark. London is a good fallback for a comparison dataset.

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Several public datasets fit, and some need no application. I'm going from memory and haven't checked the live pages, so confirm the current terms and sizes before you build on them.

## Direct download, no application

| Dataset | Households | Resolution | History | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **GoiEner** (Basque Country/Navarre, Spain; Zenodo) | ~25,000 | Hourly | Up to several years per customer | Probably the best match for your spec. It's recent and large, with metadata such as tariff and postcode. I believe the license is CC-BY. |
| **UKPN Low Carbon London** (London Datastore and Kaggle as "smart meters in London") | ~5,500 | 30 min | Nov 2011 – Feb 2014 | Coverage varies by household, and about 1,100 were on a dynamic time-of-use tariff. Joining weather is easy. |
| **Ausgrid Solar Home** (Ausgrid website) | 300 | 30 min | 3 years | Every home has rooftop PV, so consumption is confounded by solar. It may suit you or not. |
| **IDEAL** (Edinburgh DataShare) | ~255 UK homes | Down to 1 s (aggregate electricity) | ~1 year each | Rich, with sensor data and survey data, but you'd need to resample. |
| **Smart Grid Smart City** (data.gov.au) | ~78,000 | 30 min | 2010–2014 | Large and messy, with varying coverage. |

## Application required

- **Irish CER Smart Metering Project** (ISSDA): ~5,000 homes and SMEs at 30 min for about 500 days. It's free for research, but you sign a request form and agreement, and approval usually takes days. It's the classic forecasting benchmark, but it's slightly under 2 years and mixes in businesses, so filter to residential.
- **Pecan Street Dataport**: homes in Austin and elsewhere at 1-min resolution, with circuit-level data. Academic access is free but needs registration and approval. Commercial use is paid.

## Not a fit

- **UCI ElectricityLoadDiagrams** (370 clients) is mostly commercial.
- **REFIT** and **UK-DALE** have only ~5–20 homes.
- **BDG2** is buildings, not homes.

## Suggestion

Start with **GoiEner** for scale and recency, and add **London** for a second, independent test. Apply to ISSDA in parallel if you want the standard literature benchmark, since the wait is the only cost.

For day-ahead work you'll need to join weather (for example ERA5 or Open-Meteo) and holiday calendars yourself. None of these ships with them, except partly London.

I can fetch and inspect one of these (household counts, gaps, per-home history length) if you'd like me to verify it against your criteria.

Start with **GoiEner** for scale and recency, and add **London** for a second, independent test. Apply to ISSDA in parallel if you want the standard literature benchmark, since the wait is the only cost.

For day-ahead work you'll need to join weather (for example ERA5 or Open-Meteo) and holiday calendars yourself. None of these ships with them, except partly London.

I can fetch and inspect one of these (household counts, gaps, per-home history length) if you'd like me to verify it against your criteria.
