Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Several public datasets fit, and some need no application. I'm going from memory and haven't checked the live pages, so confirm the current terms and sizes before you build on them.

## Direct download, no application

| Dataset | Households | Resolution | History | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **GoiEner** (Basque Country/Navarre, Spain; Zenodo) | ~25,000 | Hourly | Up to several years per customer | Probably the best match for your spec. It's recent and large, with metadata such as tariff and postcode. I believe the license is CC-BY. |
| **UKPN Low Carbon London** (London Datastore and Kaggle as "smart meters in London") | ~5,500 | 30 min | Nov 2011 – Feb 2014 | Coverage varies by household, and about 1,100 were on a dynamic time-of-use tariff. Joining weather is easy. |
| **Ausgrid Solar Home** (Ausgrid website) | 300 | 30 min | 3 years | Every home has rooftop PV, so consumption is confounded by solar. It may suit you or not. |
| **IDEAL** (Edinburgh DataShare) | ~255 UK homes | Down to 1 s (aggregate electricity) | ~1 year each | Rich, with sensor data and survey data, but you'd need to resample. |
| **Smart Grid Smart City** (data.gov.au) | ~78,000 | 30 min | 2010–2014 | Large and messy, with varying coverage. |

## Application required

- **Irish CER Smart Metering Project** (ISSDA): ~5,000 homes and SMEs at 30 min for about 500 days. It's free for research, but you sign a request form and agreement, and approval usually takes days. It's the classic forecasting benchmark, but it's slightly under 2 years and mixes in businesses, so filter to residential.
- **Pecan Street Dataport**: homes in Austin and elsewhere at 1-min resolution, with circuit-level data. Academic access is free but needs registration and approval. Commercial use is paid.

## Not a fit

- **UCI ElectricityLoadDiagrams** (370 clients) is mostly commercial.
- **REFIT** and **UK-DALE** have only ~5–20 homes.
- **BDG2** is buildings, not homes.

## Suggestion

Start with **GoiEner** for scale and recency, and add **London** for a second, independent test. Apply to ISSDA in parallel if you want the standard literature benchmark, since the wait is the only cost.

For day-ahead work you'll need to join weather (for example ERA5 or Open-Meteo) and holiday calendars yourself. None of these ships with them, except partly London.

I can fetch and inspect one of these (household counts, gaps, per-home history length) if you'd like me to verify it against your criteria.

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I haven't checked any of the links or current access terms. Everything below is from memory, so confirm sizes, licences and URLs on each landing page before you commit.

## Meets your criteria

| Dataset | Homes | Resolution | History | Access |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **GoiEner** (Basque Country, Spain; Zenodo, Scientific Data paper) | ~25,000 | Hourly | Up to several years per customer | **Direct download**, CC-BY |
| **Low Carbon London** (UK Power Networks; London Datastore and Kaggle mirror) | ~5,500 | 30 min | ~2.25 years total (Nov 2011 to Feb 2014), shorter for some homes | **Direct download**, open licence |
| **Irish CER Smart Metering Project** (ISSDA) | ~4,200 residential, plus SMEs | 30 min | ~1.5 years (2009 to 2010) | **Application required**: a form and a data-use agreement. It's free for research, and approval usually takes days. |
| **SGSC Smart Grid Smart City** (Australia; data.gov.au) | Tens of thousands of customers | 30 min | Up to ~3 years, but coverage varies | **Direct download**. The files are large and messy. |
| **Ausgrid Solar Home** (Sydney) | 300 | 30 min | 3 years | **Direct download** from Ausgrid |

## Caveats for day-ahead benchmarking

- **GoiEner:** Start here if you want no application and the most scale. It's the most recent of these, and it has real metadata such as tariff and postcode area. Check how complete each customer's history is, because lengths vary a lot.
- **Low Carbon London:** About 1,100 homes were on a dynamic time-of-use tariff, with prices signalled a day ahead. That affects load, so filter on the standard-tariff group if you want a clean baseline. Many households also have under a year of data.
- **Irish CER:** It's the classic benchmark, so your results are easy to compare with published ones. Timestamps use an odd day-plus-slot encoding, and the dataset mixes in businesses.
- **Ausgrid:** Every home has rooftop solar. Use the gross-load series if you want consumption rather than net load. You can still use it, but it's a different problem from forecasting ordinary homes.

## Common but not a fit

- **UCI ElectricityLoadDiagrams (370 clients, Portugal, 15 min):** Very common in forecasting papers, but it mixes households and businesses and isn't clearly residential.
- **REFIT, UK-DALE, Smart\*, IDEAL:** These have far fewer homes. REFIT has 20 and UK-DALE has 5. IDEAL has about 255 UK homes and is the largest of the group, but it's sensor-oriented and short per home.
- **Pecan Street Dataport:** It has 1-minute, high-quality data. Only a small free academic tier is available, and larger access needs a paid or negotiated agreement.
- **NREL ResStock / EULP:** The loads are simulated, not measured.

## Suggested plan

1. Start with GoiEner and Low Carbon London, since neither needs an application.
2. Submit the ISSDA request now for the CER data, since approval takes time.
3. Use Ausgrid or SGSC as an extra out-of-distribution check.

Should I check the current download links and licences, or pull one of the open datasets into a working directory?

| **SGSC Smart Grid Smart City** (Australia; data.gov.au) | Tens of thousands of customers | 30 min | Up to ~3 years, but coverage varies | **Direct download**. The files are large and messy. |
| **Ausgrid Solar Home** (Sydney) | 300 | 30 min | 3 years | **Direct download** from Ausgrid |

## Caveats for day-ahead benchmarking

- **GoiEner:** Start here if you want no application and the most scale. It's the most recent of these, and it has real metadata such as tariff and postcode area. Check how complete each customer's history is, because lengths vary a lot.
- **Low Carbon London:** About 1,100 homes were on a dynamic time-of-use tariff, with prices signalled a day ahead. That affects load, so filter on the standard-tariff group if you want a clean baseline. Many households also have under a year of data.
- **Irish CER:** It's the classic benchmark, so your results are easy to compare with published ones. Timestamps use an odd day-plus-slot encoding, and the dataset mixes in businesses.
- **Ausgrid:** Every home has rooftop solar. Use the gross-load series if you want consumption rather than net load. You can still use it, but it's a different problem from forecasting ordinary homes.

## Common but not a fit

- **UCI ElectricityLoadDiagrams (370 clients, Portugal, 15 min):** Very common in forecasting papers, but it mixes households and businesses and isn't clearly residential.
- **REFIT, UK-DALE, Smart\*, IDEAL:** These have far fewer homes. REFIT has 20 and UK-DALE has 5. IDEAL has about 255 UK homes and is the largest of the group, but it's sensor-oriented and short per home.
- **Pecan Street Dataport:** It has 1-minute, high-quality data. Only a small free academic tier is available, and larger access needs a paid or negotiated agreement.
- **NREL ResStock / EULP:** The loads are simulated, not measured.

## Suggested plan

1. Start with GoiEner and Low Carbon London, since neither needs an application.
2. Submit the ISSDA request now for the CER data, since approval takes time.
3. Use Ausgrid or SGSC as an extra out-of-distribution check.

Should I check the current download links and licences, or pull one of the open datasets into a working directory?

Should I check the current download links and licences, or pull one of the open datasets into a working directory?
Prompt

I want to benchmark day-ahead electricity demand forecasting for individual homes, so I need smart-meter consumption data per household, not feeder or grid totals. Ideally hundreds of households or more, at least a year of history each, at hourly resolution or finer. What public datasets are there, and can I just download them or do I have to apply?

Graders — what "good" means for this case
00-skill-firedtool_used
tool"Skill"
input_match"\"skill\"\\s*:\\s*\"(?:[\\w-]+:)?dataset-scout\""
01-recall-low-carbon-londonllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the London smart meter dataset from the Low Carbon London project (also known as "SmartMeter Energy Consumption Data in London Households", LCL, the UK Power Networks London smart meter data on the London Datastore, or the Kaggle "Smart meters in London" copy) as a candidate household electricity dataset. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

02-recall-cer-irish-smart-meterllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the Irish CER Smart Metering Project electricity data (also known as the CER Electricity Customer Behaviour Trial 2009-2010, the Irish Commission for Energy Regulation smart meter data, or the ISSDA smart meter data) as a candidate household electricity dataset. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

03-recall-smart-grid-smart-cityllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the Smart-Grid Smart-City Customer Trial Data (also known as SGSC, the Ausgrid-led Smart Grid Smart City trial from New South Wales, Australia, published on data.gov.au) as a candidate household electricity dataset. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

04-recall-pecan-streetllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists Pecan Street Dataport (also known as Pecan Street, the Pecan Street Inc. residential energy data from Austin, Texas and other testbeds) as a candidate household electricity dataset. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

05-access-low-carbon-london-openllm

PASS if the final answer states that the Low Carbon London smart meter data ("SmartMeter Energy Consumption Data in London Households", LCL) can be downloaded directly, without an application, from the London Datastore. If the answer names its licence, the licence must be Creative Commons Attribution (CC BY, version 4.0 on the London Datastore page). FAIL if the answer does not mention this dataset, says it must be applied for, or gives a different licence (for example a non-commercial one).

06-access-cer-issda-requestllm

PASS if the final answer states that the Irish CER Smart Metering Project electricity data is not an open download but must be requested from the Irish Social Science Data Archive (ISSDA), for example by submitting an ISSDA data request form that is reviewed before access is granted, and that it is provided for non-commercial research or teaching. Mentioning the ISSDA end user licence, or that ISSDA supplies data only for use in the EEA and countries with an EU adequacy decision, is welcome but not required. FAIL if the answer does not mention the CER data, describes it as freely downloadable, or says nothing about how access to it is obtained.

07-access-pecan-street-free-tier-limitedllm

PASS if the final answer states that Pecan Street Dataport requires an account and a licence, and that free access for university researchers covers only a limited subset of homes (75 homes: 25 each from Austin, New York and California) while the full datasets require a paid licence. An answer that conveys either the limited free subset or the paid licence for the full data passes. FAIL if the answer does not mention Pecan Street, describes the full dataset as freely downloadable or free for all academic use without any limitation, or says nothing about how access to it is obtained.

08-access-smart-grid-smart-city-openllm

PASS if the final answer states that the Smart-Grid Smart-City (SGSC) Customer Trial Data can be downloaded directly, without an application, from data.gov.au. If the answer names its licence, the licence must be Creative Commons Attribution (CC BY 3.0 Australia). FAIL if the answer does not mention this dataset, says it must be applied for, or gives a different licence.

09-trap-uci-individual-householdllm

PASS if the final answer does not present the UCI "Individual household electric power consumption" dataset (one-minute measurements from a single house in Sceaux near Paris, December 2006 to November 2010) as meeting the requirement of many households. Not mentioning it passes. Mentioning it while saying that it covers only one household passes. FAIL if the answer recommends or lists this dataset as a multi-household dataset, or lists it as a candidate for this many-household benchmark without saying that it contains a single household.

10-long-tailllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists at least one of these less widely known household electricity datasets: the IDEAL household energy dataset (255 homes in and around Edinburgh, Scotland, on Edinburgh DataShare); or the GoiEner smart meter data (hourly consumption from customers of the Spanish electricity cooperative GoiEner, on Zenodo). FAIL if the answer mentions neither of them.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
**Short answer:** you can start today on the PhysioNet/CinC Challenge 2019 data, which has a time-stamped sepsis label and needs no application. The larger datasets (MIMIC-IV, eICU, HiRID and others) need PhysioNet credentialing, which PhysioNet's FAQ puts at about 1–2 weeks. That is tight for a one-week deadline, so start the application today.

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** a time-stamped sepsis onset label, or data from which you can derive Sepsis-3 onset (suspected infection plus a SOFA rise).
- **R2:** adult ICU vitals and labs.
- **R3 (nice to have):** access fast enough for a baseline within a week.
- I assumed an academic, non-commercial project.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | [PhysioNet/CinC Challenge 2019](https://physionet.org/content/challenge-2019/1.0.0/) | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | Strong: label already provided | Registry today; label details from memory |
| 2 | [MIMIC-IV](https://physionet.org/content/mimiciv/) (v3.1) | Credentialed, ~1–2 weeks | PhysioNet Credentialed Health Data License | Strong: derive onset yourself | Today |
| 3 | [eICU-CRD](https://physionet.org/content/eicu-crd/) | Credentialed, same approval as MIMIC | Credentialed license | Strong for multi-centre validation; derive onset | Today |
| 4 | [HiRID](https://physionet.org/content/hirid/1.1.1/) | Credentialed plus approval from the data contributors | Contributor Review license | Strong for temporal models; derive onset | Today |
| 5 | [SICdb](https://physionet.org/content/sicdb/1.0.8/) | Credentialed plus contributor approval | Contributor Review license | Partial: external validation | Today |
| 6 | [NWICU](https://doi.org/10.13026/4je9-db46) | Credentialed | Credentialed license | Unknown: I didn't check whether it has enough infection data | Today (access only) |
| 7 | [MIMIC-III](https://physionet.org/content/mimiciii/1.4/) | Credentialed | Credentialed license | Partial: superseded by MIMIC-IV | Today |
| 8 | [MIMIC-IV Demo](https://physionet.org/content/mimic-iv-demo/2.2/) | Open | ODbL-1.0 | Weak: too small to train on | Today |

## Details
**1. Challenge 2019** is your week-one baseline.
- The page confirms 40,336 subjects, open access and CC-BY-4.0.
- From memory, not confirmed today: it has hourly records from two hospital systems, a per-hour `SepsisLabel` based on Sepsis-3, and about 40 vitals, lab and demographic columns.
- The limit is that the label is fixed. You can't change the onset definition, and the variable set is narrow.

**2–3. MIMIC-IV and eICU** share one access route.
1. Create a PhysioNet account.
2. Complete the CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" course. Per PhysioNet's instructions, affiliate with "Massachusetts Institute of Technology Affiliates" and avoid registering as an independent learner, to skip fees.
3. Submit the credentialing application with your CITI completion report. It asks about you and your research. I didn't confirm the reference details, so check `physionet.org/settings/profile/`.
4. Once approved, sign the data use agreement on each dataset page. Sharing data within a team is not permitted, so each of your collaborators needs their own approval.
- Neither dataset ships a Sepsis-3 onset label. Both contain the antibiotic, culture, lab and vitals data you need to derive it. The MIT-LCP `mimic-code` repository has Sepsis-3 derivations for MIMIC. That is from memory and I did not check it this session.
- Check the agreement before using any online AI service on this data. PhysioNet's terms restrict that.

**4–5. HiRID and SICdb** need credentialing and a separate request to the data contributors.
- HiRID's page says you must "submit a request to the authors to use the data for your project" and "provide a specific research question".
- SICdb's page says contributors' approval is required.
- Plan for extra time on top of credentialing, and I can't give you an estimate for it. They're best used as external validation sets.

**AmsterdamUMCdb** is worth a mention. A web search says access is free but needs a CITI-type course, a signed agreement and a named reference, and the licence is non-commercial. It is credible but unconfirmed. See the next section.

## Known, not confirmed
- **AmsterdamUMCdb.** I couldn't verify a record page. My guessed URL returned 404 and I did not use it. The site that the search returned is [amsterdammedicaldatascience.nl](https://amsterdammedicaldatascience.nl/). The search result also said the current version is 1.5.0 (OMOP format), and that you request access there and get the data via DANS. Check that site for the current form and whether it has what you need for onset.

## Not recommended
- **WiDS Datathon 2020:** the label is mortality, so there are no onset times.
- **Zenodo/Dryad sepsis records** from the registry search: these are small biomarker or study datasets, not ICU time series.

## What I could not find
- No other open dataset with sepsis onset labels beyond Challenge 2019.
- The literature search mostly timed out or returned irrelevant results, so I can't point you to confirmed papers that used these datasets. You'll find plenty by searching the Challenge 2019 and MIMIC-IV Sepsis-3 literature yourself.
- Registry search covered PhysioNet, Zenodo, OpenAIRE and DataCite. Lesser-known sources may be missing.

## Next step
1. Today: download Challenge 2019 and build the baseline.
2. Today: start the CITI course, create your PhysioNet account and submit the credentialing application. Request MIMIC-IV and eICU, and HiRID if you want it.
3. While you wait: develop your Sepsis-3 derivation on the open MIMIC-IV demo, so it's ready when the full data arrives.

Access terms were checked on 2026-11-11/12, and they can change. The interactive page is at `/private/tmp/e-kOXwcR/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`. Some of its per-requirement evidence text is my own summary, so treat the reply above as authoritative.

Sources: [PhysioNet FAQs](https://physionet.org/about/faqs/), [PhysioNet CITI instructions](https://physionet.org/about/citi-course/), [AmsterdamUMCdb on GitHub](https://github.com/AmsterdamUMC/AmsterdamUMCdb)

- Registry search covered PhysioNet, Zenodo, OpenAIRE and DataCite. Lesser-known sources may be missing.

## Next step
1. Today: download Challenge 2019 and build the baseline.
2. Today: start the CITI course, create your PhysioNet account and submit the credentialing application. Request MIMIC-IV and eICU, and HiRID if you want it.
3. While you wait: develop your Sepsis-3 derivation on the open MIMIC-IV demo, so it's ready when the full data arrives.

Access terms were checked on 2026-11-11/12, and they can change. The interactive page is at `/private/tmp/e-kOXwcR/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`. Some of its per-requirement evidence text is my own summary, so treat the reply above as authoritative.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
**Short answer:** you can start today on the PhysioNet/CinC Challenge 2019 data, which has a time-stamped sepsis label and needs no application. The larger datasets (MIMIC-IV, eICU, HiRID and others) need PhysioNet credentialing, which PhysioNet's FAQ puts at about 1–2 weeks. That is tight for a one-week deadline, so start the application today.

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** a time-stamped sepsis onset label, or data from which you can derive Sepsis-3 onset (suspected infection plus a SOFA rise).
- **R2:** adult ICU vitals and labs.
- **R3 (nice to have):** access fast enough for a baseline within a week.
- I assumed an academic, non-commercial project.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | [PhysioNet/CinC Challenge 2019](https://physionet.org/content/challenge-2019/1.0.0/) | Open download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | Strong: label already provided | Registry today; label details from memory |
| 2 | [MIMIC-IV](https://physionet.org/content/mimiciv/) (v3.1) | Credentialed, ~1–2 weeks | PhysioNet Credentialed Health Data License | Strong: derive onset yourself | Today |
| 3 | [eICU-CRD](https://physionet.org/content/eicu-crd/) | Credentialed, same approval as MIMIC | Credentialed license | Strong for multi-centre validation; derive onset | Today |
| 4 | [HiRID](https://physionet.org/content/hirid/1.1.1/) | Credentialed plus approval from the data contributors | Contributor Review license | Strong for temporal models; derive onset | Today |
| 5 | [SICdb](https://physionet.org/content/sicdb/1.0.8/) | Credentialed plus contributor approval | Contributor Review license | Partial: external validation | Today |
| 6 | [NWICU](https://doi.org/10.13026/4je9-db46) | Credentialed | Credentialed license | Unknown: I didn't check whether it has enough infection data | Today (access only) |
| 7 | [MIMIC-III](https://physionet.org/content/mimiciii/1.4/) | Credentialed | Credentialed license | Partial: superseded by MIMIC-IV | Today |
| 8 | [MIMIC-IV Demo](https://physionet.org/content/mimic-iv-demo/2.2/) | Open | ODbL-1.0 | Weak: too small to train on | Today |

## Details
**1. Challenge 2019** is your week-one baseline.
- The page confirms 40,336 subjects, open access and CC-BY-4.0.
- From memory, not confirmed today: it has hourly records from two hospital systems, a per-hour `SepsisLabel` based on Sepsis-3, and about 40 vitals, lab and demographic columns.
- The limit is that the label is fixed. You can't change the onset definition, and the variable set is narrow.

**2–3. MIMIC-IV and eICU** share one access route.
1. Create a PhysioNet account.
2. Complete the CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" course. Per PhysioNet's instructions, affiliate with "Massachusetts Institute of Technology Affiliates" and avoid registering as an independent learner, to skip fees.
3. Submit the credentialing application with your CITI completion report. It asks about you and your research. I didn't confirm the reference details, so check `physionet.org/settings/profile/`.
4. Once approved, sign the data use agreement on each dataset page. Sharing data within a team is not permitted, so each of your collaborators needs their own approval.
- Neither dataset ships a Sepsis-3 onset label. Both contain the antibiotic, culture, lab and vitals data you need to derive it. The MIT-LCP `mimic-code` repository has Sepsis-3 derivations for MIMIC. That is from memory and I did not check it this session.
- Check the agreement before using any online AI service on this data. PhysioNet's terms restrict that.

**4–5. HiRID and SICdb** need credentialing and a separate request to the data contributors.
- HiRID's page says you must "submit a request to the authors to use the data for your project" and "provide a specific research question".
- SICdb's page says contributors' approval is required.
- Plan for extra time on top of credentialing, and I can't give you an estimate for it. They're best used as external validation sets.

**AmsterdamUMCdb** is worth a mention. A web search says access is free but needs a CITI-type course, a signed agreement and a named reference, and the licence is non-commercial. It is credible but unconfirmed. See the next section.

## Known, not confirmed
- **AmsterdamUMCdb.** I couldn't verify a record page. My guessed URL returned 404 and I did not use it. The site that the search returned is [amsterdammedicaldatascience.nl](https://amsterdammedicaldatascience.nl/). The search result also said the current version is 1.5.0 (OMOP format), and that you request access there and get the data via DANS. Check that site for the current form and whether it has what you need for onset.

## Not recommended
- **WiDS Datathon 2020:** the label is mortality, so there are no onset times.
- **Zenodo/Dryad sepsis records** from the registry search: these are small biomarker or study datasets, not ICU time series.

## What I could not find
- No other open dataset with sepsis onset labels beyond Challenge 2019.
- The literature search mostly timed out or returned irrelevant results, so I can't point you to confirmed papers that used these datasets. You'll find plenty by searching the Challenge 2019 and MIMIC-IV Sepsis-3 literature yourself.
- Registry search covered PhysioNet, Zenodo, OpenAIRE and DataCite. Lesser-known sources may be missing.

## Next step
1. Today: download Challenge 2019 and build the baseline.
2. Today: start the CITI course, create your PhysioNet account and submit the credentialing application. Request MIMIC-IV and eICU, and HiRID if you want it.
3. While you wait: develop your Sepsis-3 derivation on the open MIMIC-IV demo, so it's ready when the full data arrives.

| 5 | [SICdb](https://physionet.org/content/sicdb/1.0.8/) | Credentialed plus contributor approval | Contributor Review license | Partial: external validation | Today |
| 6 | [NWICU](https://doi.org/10.13026/4je9-db46) | Credentialed | Credentialed license | Unknown: I didn't check whether it has enough infection data | Today (access only) |
| 7 | [MIMIC-III](https://physionet.org/content/mimiciii/1.4/) | Credentialed | Credentialed license | Partial: superseded by MIMIC-IV | Today |
| 8 | [MIMIC-IV Demo](https://physionet.org/content/mimic-iv-demo/2.2/) | Open | ODbL-1.0 | Weak: too small to train on | Today |

## Details
**1. Challenge 2019** is your week-one baseline.
- The page confirms 40,336 subjects, open access and CC-BY-4.0.
- From memory, not confirmed today: it has hourly records from two hospital systems, a per-hour `SepsisLabel` based on Sepsis-3, and about 40 vitals, lab and demographic columns.
- The limit is that the label is fixed. You can't change the onset definition, and the variable set is narrow.

**2–3. MIMIC-IV and eICU** share one access route.
1. Create a PhysioNet account.
2. Complete the CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" course. Per PhysioNet's instructions, affiliate with "Massachusetts Institute of Technology Affiliates" and avoid registering as an independent learner, to skip fees.
3. Submit the credentialing application with your CITI completion report. It asks about you and your research. I didn't confirm the reference details, so check `physionet.org/settings/profile/`.
4. Once approved, sign the data use agreement on each dataset page. Sharing data within a team is not permitted, so each of your collaborators needs their own approval.
- Neither dataset ships a Sepsis-3 onset label. Both contain the antibiotic, culture, lab and vitals data you need to derive it. The MIT-LCP `mimic-code` repository has Sepsis-3 derivations for MIMIC. That is from memory and I did not check it this session.
- Check the agreement before using any online AI service on this data. PhysioNet's terms restrict that.

**4–5. HiRID and SICdb** need credentialing and a separate request to the data contributors.
- HiRID's page says you must "submit a request to the authors to use the data for your project" and "provide a specific research question".
- SICdb's page says contributors' approval is required.
- Plan for extra time on top of credentialing, and I can't give you an estimate for it. They're best used as external validation sets.

**AmsterdamUMCdb** is worth a mention. A web search says access is free but needs a CITI-type course, a signed agreement and a named reference, and the licence is non-commercial. It is credible but unconfirmed. See the next section.

## Known, not confirmed
- **AmsterdamUMCdb.** I couldn't verify a record page. My guessed URL returned 404 and I did not use it. The site that the search returned is [amsterdammedicaldatascience.nl](https://amsterdammedicaldatascience.nl/). The search result also said the current version is 1.5.0 (OMOP format), and that you request access there and get the data via DANS. Check that site for the current form and whether it has what you need for onset.

## Not recommended
- **WiDS Datathon 2020:** the label is mortality, so there are no onset times.
- **Zenodo/Dryad sepsis records** from the registry search: these are small biomarker or study datasets, not ICU time series.

## What I could not find
- No other open dataset with sepsis onset labels beyond Challenge 2019.
- The literature search mostly timed out or returned irrelevant results, so I can't point you to confirmed papers that used these datasets. You'll find plenty by searching the Challenge 2019 and MIMIC-IV Sepsis-3 literature yourself.
- Registry search covered PhysioNet, Zenodo, OpenAIRE and DataCite. Lesser-known sources may be missing.

## Next step
1. Today: download Challenge 2019 and build the baseline.
2. Today: start the CITI course, create your PhysioNet account and submit the credentialing application. Request MIMIC-IV and eICU, and HiRID if you want it.
3. While you wait: develop your Sepsis-3 derivation on the open MIMIC-IV demo, so it's ready when the full data arrives.

Access terms were checked on 2026-11-11/12, and they can change. The interactive page is at `/private/tmp/e-kOXwcR/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`. Some of its per-requirement evidence text is my own summary, so treat the reply above as authoritative.

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
## What I assumed you need

- **R1 (must):** adult ICU time series of vitals and labs.
- **R2 (must):** a time-stamped sepsis onset label, or the data to derive Sepsis-3 onset yourself (SOFA change plus suspected infection).
- **R3 (must):** thousands of patients.
- **R4 (nice to have):** usable within about a week.

I assumed you'll work in Python and don't already hold PhysioNet credentials. If you already have credentials, the timelines below get much shorter.

## Shortlist

All six are hosted on PhysioNet, so one account and one training course cover most of the applications.

| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | [PhysioNet/CinC Challenge 2019 (sepsis)](https://physionet.org/content/challenge-2019/1.0.0/) | **Open download, minutes** | CC-BY-4.0 | 40,336 subjects | Strong | today |
| 2 | [MIMIC-IV](https://physionet.org/content/mimiciv/) | Credentialed, ~1–3 weeks | PhysioNet Credentialed Health Data License | very large | Strong (onset must be derived) | today (size from memory) |
| 3 | [eICU-CRD](https://physionet.org/content/eicu-crd/2.0/) | Credentialed, same process as MIMIC-IV | PhysioNet Credentialed license | multicentre, large | Strong (onset must be derived) | today (size from memory) |
| 4 | [HiRID](https://physionet.org/content/hirid/1.1.1/) | Credentialed plus approval from the data authors | PhysioNet Contributor Review license | ~34k admissions | Strong (onset must be derived) | today |
| 5 | [SICdb](https://physionet.org/content/sicdb/1.0.8/) | Credentialed plus approval from the data authors | PhysioNet Contributor Review license | not confirmed | Partial | today |
| 6 | [NWICU](https://physionet.org/content/nwicu-northwestern-icu/0.1.0/) | Credentialed | PhysioNet Credentialed license | not checked | Partial (contents not checked) | today (access only) |

## Details

**1. Challenge 2019: start here.**
- The page says: "we define sepsis according to the Sepsis-3 guidelines, i.e., a two-point change in the patient's SOFA score and clinical suspicion of infection (as defined by the ordering of blood cultures or IV antibiotics)."
- It gives you hourly vitals and labs with a per-hour sepsis label, and its utility score rewards early predictions and penalizes late ones, so you can reproduce published baselines directly.
- The label is the organisers' approximation, so read their onset convention before you trust the lead times.
- The test set is hidden, so you split the training data yourself.
- It combines two hospital systems. The "adult only" detail is from memory.
- Many sepsis papers use it. One returned by the literature search is an [LSTM-GAT model on this dataset](https://doi.org/10.1136/bmjhci-2025-101762).

**2 and 3. MIMIC-IV and eICU-CRD.** These have no ready-made Sepsis-3 label. You derive onset from SOFA components plus antibiotic and culture times. That derivation is a project in itself, but it is what makes your labels defensible, and it lets you validate across the two datasets. The steps:
1. Create a PhysioNet account.
2. Take the CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" course. PhysioNet's instructions say to upload the training report, not the certificate, and to affiliate with MIT to avoid fees.
3. Submit the credentialing application. The reviewers are PhysioNet staff, and I don't know their current turnaround (my "1–3 weeks" is from memory).
4. Sign the data use agreement on each project page. MIMIC-IV's page says: "Only credentialed users who sign the DUA can access the files."

Credentialing is done once and covers both datasets. eICU-CRD timestamps are minute offsets from unit admission.

**4 and 5. HiRID and SICdb.** You need credentialing first. After that you submit a request to the data authors with "a specific research question" and sign their agreement. HiRID has 2-minute resolution from a Swiss ICU. Both are good for external validation, but I wouldn't plan the first week around them.

**6. NWICU.** It's a newer US ICU database at version 0.1.0. I only confirmed its access tier. Check its contents before relying on it.

## Known, not confirmed

- **AmsterdamUMCdb.** I recall it as a Dutch ICU database that needs an application form and a signed agreement through Amsterdam UMC. The page I tried returned a 404, so I couldn't confirm that, or its current terms. Search for the current official page before you plan around it.
- **Sizes and the derivation code.** The MIMIC-IV and eICU sizes, and the community SOFA and Sepsis-3 derivation code for MIMIC, are from memory.

## Not recommended, and why

- **Synthetic sepsis datasets** (for example [this figshare record](https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.32931644)). They contain no real patients, so they're no use for a research baseline.
- **Datasets that only hold mortality or discharge codes.** These came up in the registry noise. They fail R2.

## What I could not find

- The Zenodo and DataCite searches partly failed, so small institutional sepsis datasets may be missing.
- I didn't pull per-dataset usage papers beyond the one literature search.

## Next step

1. **Today:** download Challenge 2019 and build the baseline. This meets the one-week deadline on its own.
2. **Today:** start the PhysioNet account and the CITI course, since credentialing is the long pole.
3. **After approval:** apply for HiRID and SICdb, and derive Sepsis-3 labels on MIMIC-IV and eICU for external validation.

## What I assumed you need

- **R1 (must):** adult ICU time series of vitals and labs.
- **R2 (must):** a time-stamped sepsis onset label, or the data to derive Sepsis-3 onset yourself (SOFA change plus suspected infection).
- **R3 (must):** thousands of patients.
- **R4 (nice to have):** usable within about a week.

I assumed you'll work in Python and don't already hold PhysioNet credentials. If you already have credentials, the timelines below get much shorter.

## Shortlist

All six are hosted on PhysioNet, so one account and one training course cover most of the applications.

| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | [PhysioNet/CinC Challenge 2019 (sepsis)](https://physionet.org/content/challenge-2019/1.0.0/) | **Open download, minutes** | CC-BY-4.0 | 40,336 subjects | Strong | today |
| 2 | [MIMIC-IV](https://physionet.org/content/mimiciv/) | Credentialed, ~1–3 weeks | PhysioNet Credentialed Health Data License | very large | Strong (onset must be derived) | today (size from memory) |
| 3 | [eICU-CRD](https://physionet.org/content/eicu-crd/2.0/) | Credentialed, same process as MIMIC-IV | PhysioNet Credentialed license | multicentre, large | Strong (onset must be derived) | today (size from memory) |
| 4 | [HiRID](https://physionet.org/content/hirid/1.1.1/) | Credentialed plus approval from the data authors | PhysioNet Contributor Review license | ~34k admissions | Strong (onset must be derived) | today |
| 5 | [SICdb](https://physionet.org/content/sicdb/1.0.8/) | Credentialed plus approval from the data authors | PhysioNet Contributor Review license | not confirmed | Partial | today |
| 6 | [NWICU](https://physionet.org/content/nwicu-northwestern-icu/0.1.0/) | Credentialed | PhysioNet Credentialed license | not checked | Partial (contents not checked) | today (access only) |

## Details

**1. Challenge 2019: start here.**
- The page says: "we define sepsis according to the Sepsis-3 guidelines, i.e., a two-point change in the patient's SOFA score and clinical suspicion of infection (as defined by the ordering of blood cultures or IV antibiotics)."
- It gives you hourly vitals and labs with a per-hour sepsis label, and its utility score rewards early predictions and penalizes late ones, so you can reproduce published baselines directly.
- The label is the organisers' approximation, so read their onset convention before you trust the lead times.
- The test set is hidden, so you split the training data yourself.
- It combines two hospital systems. The "adult only" detail is from memory.
- Many sepsis papers use it. One returned by the literature search is an [LSTM-GAT model on this dataset](https://doi.org/10.1136/bmjhci-2025-101762).

**2 and 3. MIMIC-IV and eICU-CRD.** These have no ready-made Sepsis-3 label. You derive onset from SOFA components plus antibiotic and culture times. That derivation is a project in itself, but it is what makes your labels defensible, and it lets you validate across the two datasets. The steps:
1. Create a PhysioNet account.
2. Take the CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" course. PhysioNet's instructions say to upload the training report, not the certificate, and to affiliate with MIT to avoid fees.
3. Submit the credentialing application. The reviewers are PhysioNet staff, and I don't know their current turnaround (my "1–3 weeks" is from memory).
4. Sign the data use agreement on each project page. MIMIC-IV's page says: "Only credentialed users who sign the DUA can access the files."

Credentialing is done once and covers both datasets. eICU-CRD timestamps are minute offsets from unit admission.

**4 and 5. HiRID and SICdb.** You need credentialing first. After that you submit a request to the data authors with "a specific research question" and sign their agreement. HiRID has 2-minute resolution from a Swiss ICU. Both are good for external validation, but I wouldn't plan the first week around them.

**6. NWICU.** It's a newer US ICU database at version 0.1.0. I only confirmed its access tier. Check its contents before relying on it.

## Known, not confirmed

- **AmsterdamUMCdb.** I recall it as a Dutch ICU database that needs an application form and a signed agreement through Amsterdam UMC. The page I tried returned a 404, so I couldn't confirm that, or its current terms. Search for the current official page before you plan around it.
- **Sizes and the derivation code.** The MIMIC-IV and eICU sizes, and the community SOFA and Sepsis-3 derivation code for MIMIC, are from memory.

## Not recommended, and why

- **Synthetic sepsis datasets** (for example [this figshare record](https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.32931644)). They contain no real patients, so they're no use for a research baseline.
- **Datasets that only hold mortality or discharge codes.** These came up in the registry noise. They fail R2.

## What I could not find

- The Zenodo and DataCite searches partly failed, so small institutional sepsis datasets may be missing.
- I didn't pull per-dataset usage papers beyond the one literature search.

## Next step

1. **Today:** download Challenge 2019 and build the baseline. This meets the one-week deadline on its own.
2. **Today:** start the PhysioNet account and the CITI course, since credentialing is the long pole.
3. **After approval:** apply for HiRID and SICdb, and derive Sepsis-3 labels on MIMIC-IV and eICU for external validation.

- **AmsterdamUMCdb.** I recall it as a Dutch ICU database that needs an application form and a signed agreement through Amsterdam UMC. The page I tried returned a 404, so I couldn't confirm that, or its current terms. Search for the current official page before you plan around it.
- **Sizes and the derivation code.** The MIMIC-IV and eICU sizes, and the community SOFA and Sepsis-3 derivation code for MIMIC, are from memory.

## Not recommended, and why

- **Synthetic sepsis datasets** (for example [this figshare record](https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.32931644)). They contain no real patients, so they're no use for a research baseline.
- **Datasets that only hold mortality or discharge codes.** These came up in the registry noise. They fail R2.

## What I could not find

- The Zenodo and DataCite searches partly failed, so small institutional sepsis datasets may be missing.
- I didn't pull per-dataset usage papers beyond the one literature search.

## Next step

1. **Today:** download Challenge 2019 and build the baseline. This meets the one-week deadline on its own.
2. **Today:** start the PhysioNet account and the CITI course, since credentialing is the long pole.
3. **After approval:** apply for HiRID and SICdb, and derive Sepsis-3 labels on MIMIC-IV and eICU for external validation.

## What I assumed you need

- **R1 (must):** adult ICU time series of vitals and labs.
- **R2 (must):** a time-stamped sepsis onset label, or the data to derive Sepsis-3 onset yourself (SOFA change plus suspected infection).
- **R3 (must):** thousands of patients.
- **R4 (nice to have):** usable within about a week.

I assumed you'll work in Python and don't already hold PhysioNet credentials. If you already have credentials, the timelines below get much shorter.

## Shortlist

All six are hosted on PhysioNet, so one account and one training course cover most of the applications.

| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | [PhysioNet/CinC Challenge 2019 (sepsis)](https://physionet.org/content/challenge-2019/1.0.0/) | **Open download, minutes** | CC-BY-4.0 | 40,336 subjects | Strong | today |
| 2 | [MIMIC-IV](https://physionet.org/content/mimiciv/) | Credentialed, ~1–3 weeks | PhysioNet Credentialed Health Data License | very large | Strong (onset must be derived) | today (size from memory) |
| 3 | [eICU-CRD](https://physionet.org/content/eicu-crd/2.0/) | Credentialed, same process as MIMIC-IV | PhysioNet Credentialed license | multicentre, large | Strong (onset must be derived) | today (size from memory) |
| 4 | [HiRID](https://physionet.org/content/hirid/1.1.1/) | Credentialed plus approval from the data authors | PhysioNet Contributor Review license | ~34k admissions | Strong (onset must be derived) | today |
| 5 | [SICdb](https://physionet.org/content/sicdb/1.0.8/) | Credentialed plus approval from the data authors | PhysioNet Contributor Review license | not confirmed | Partial | today |
| 6 | [NWICU](https://physionet.org/content/nwicu-northwestern-icu/0.1.0/) | Credentialed | PhysioNet Credentialed license | not checked | Partial (contents not checked) | today (access only) |

## Details

**1. Challenge 2019: start here.**
- The page says: "we define sepsis according to the Sepsis-3 guidelines, i.e., a two-point change in the patient's SOFA score and clinical suspicion of infection (as defined by the ordering of blood cultures or IV antibiotics)."
- It gives you hourly vitals and labs with a per-hour sepsis label, and its utility score rewards early predictions and penalizes late ones, so you can reproduce published baselines directly.
- The label is the organisers' approximation, so read their onset convention before you trust the lead times.
- The test set is hidden, so you split the training data yourself.
- It combines two hospital systems. The "adult only" detail is from memory.
- Many sepsis papers use it. One returned by the literature search is an [LSTM-GAT model on this dataset](https://doi.org/10.1136/bmjhci-2025-101762).

**2 and 3. MIMIC-IV and eICU-CRD.** These have no ready-made Sepsis-3 label. You derive onset from SOFA components plus antibiotic and culture times. That derivation is a project in itself, but it is what makes your labels defensible, and it lets you validate across the two datasets. The steps:
1. Create a PhysioNet account.
2. Take the CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" course. PhysioNet's instructions say to upload the training report, not the certificate, and to affiliate with MIT to avoid fees.
3. Submit the credentialing application. The reviewers are PhysioNet staff, and I don't know their current turnaround (my "1–3 weeks" is from memory).
4. Sign the data use agreement on each project page. MIMIC-IV's page says: "Only credentialed users who sign the DUA can access the files."

Credentialing is done once and covers both datasets. eICU-CRD timestamps are minute offsets from unit admission.

**4 and 5. HiRID and SICdb.** You need credentialing first. After that you submit a request to the data authors with "a specific research question" and sign their agreement. HiRID has 2-minute resolution from a Swiss ICU. Both are good for external validation, but I wouldn't plan the first week around them.

**6. NWICU.** It's a newer US ICU database at version 0.1.0. I only confirmed its access tier. Check its contents before relying on it.

## Known, not confirmed

- **AmsterdamUMCdb.** I recall it as a Dutch ICU database that needs an application form and a signed agreement through Amsterdam UMC. The page I tried returned a 404, so I couldn't confirm that, or its current terms. Search for the current official page before you plan around it.
- **Sizes and the derivation code.** The MIMIC-IV and eICU sizes, and the community SOFA and Sepsis-3 derivation code for MIMIC, are from memory.

## Not recommended, and why

- **Synthetic sepsis datasets** (for example [this figshare record](https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.32931644)). They contain no real patients, so they're no use for a research baseline.
- **Datasets that only hold mortality or discharge codes.** These came up in the registry noise. They fail R2.

## What I could not find

- The Zenodo and DataCite searches partly failed, so small institutional sepsis datasets may be missing.
- I didn't pull per-dataset usage papers beyond the one literature search.

## Next step

- **AmsterdamUMCdb.** I recall it as a Dutch ICU database that needs an application form and a signed agreement through Amsterdam UMC. The page I tried returned a 404, so I couldn't confirm that, or its current terms. Search for the current official page before you plan around it.
- **Sizes and the derivation code.** The MIMIC-IV and eICU sizes, and the community SOFA and Sepsis-3 derivation code for MIMIC, are from memory.

## Not recommended, and why

- **Synthetic sepsis datasets** (for example [this figshare record](https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.32931644)). They contain no real patients, so they're no use for a research baseline.
- **Datasets that only hold mortality or discharge codes.** These came up in the registry noise. They fail R2.

## What I could not find

- The Zenodo and DataCite searches partly failed, so small institutional sepsis datasets may be missing.
- I didn't pull per-dataset usage papers beyond the one literature search.

## Next step

1. **Today:** download Challenge 2019 and build the baseline. This meets the one-week deadline on its own.
2. **Today:** start the PhysioNet account and the CITI course, since credentialing is the long pole.
3. **After approval:** apply for HiRID and SICdb, and derive Sepsis-3 labels on MIMIC-IV and eICU for external validation.

Checked 2026-11-12. Access rules and licences change, so check the pages again before you submit anything.

## Download today, no application

**PhysioNet/Computing in Cardiology Challenge 2019 (Early Prediction of Sepsis)** is open access.
- It has about 40k ICU patients with hourly vitals and labs from two hospital systems (training sets A and B). Each hour carries a `SepsisLabel` derived from Sepsis-3 criteria.
- It comes with the official utility-score evaluator, which rewards early but not too-early predictions, so you get a standard metric and published baselines to compare against.
- Caveats: the label is a challenge-specific implementation, not a clinician-adjudicated one. The variable set is limited, and the third hospital system's test set was never released.
- This is your best fit for a baseline within a week.

## Credentialed access (PhysioNet)

All of these use one PhysioNet credentialing process, so you apply once.

- **What you do:** create an account and complete the CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" course. Then submit the application with your institutional details and a reference (your supervisor), and sign the data use agreement for each dataset.
- **How long it takes:** often a few days to a couple of weeks, depending on reviewer load. Start now, because it may not land inside your one-week window.
- **Datasets this unlocks:**
  - **MIMIC-IV** (Beth Israel Deaconess, single center): the standard choice. It has no onset column, but the MIMIC-Code repository has a `sepsis3` derived concept (suspected infection from antibiotics plus cultures, combined with SOFA ≥ 2), which gives you onset times.
  - **eICU-CRD** (multicenter US): the same credentials work. You derive Sepsis-3 labels yourself, and it's good for external validation.
  - **HiRID** (Bern, high-resolution): a credentialed PhysioNet dataset. Its sampling is dense, and you also build the labels yourself.
  - **SICdb** (Salzburg): also credentialed on PhysioNet, as far as I recall. Check its page, as it's newer.

## Separate application

- **AmsterdamUMCdb** (Amsterdam UMC) goes through the Amsterdam Data Science / UMC access process. This is typically a data access agreement with institutional sign-off and takes longer, so treat it as a later-stage external validation set, not a week-one option.

## Shortcut for labels

**YAIB (Yet Another ICU Benchmark)** includes harmonized pipelines for MIMIC, eICU, HiRID and AmsterdamUMCdb, with a sepsis prediction task and Sepsis-3-style labels. It still needs the underlying credentialed data, but it saves you from writing the onset-derivation and preprocessing code yourself.

## Suggested plan for the week

1. **Today:** start the CITI course and the PhysioNet application, and download the Challenge 2019 data.
2. **This week:** build your baseline (for example gradient-boosted trees on hourly windows with forward-filled features) on Challenge 2019 and score it with the utility metric.
3. **When credentialing clears:** move to MIMIC-IV with the `sepsis3` concept and validate on eICU.

Sepsis-3 onset definitions vary between implementations, such as the suspected-infection time window and the baseline SOFA assumption. Whichever you use, document your choices, because reviewers will ask.

## Shortcut for labels

**YAIB (Yet Another ICU Benchmark)** includes harmonized pipelines for MIMIC, eICU, HiRID and AmsterdamUMCdb, with a sepsis prediction task and Sepsis-3-style labels. It still needs the underlying credentialed data, but it saves you from writing the onset-derivation and preprocessing code yourself.

## Suggested plan for the week

1. **Today:** start the CITI course and the PhysioNet application, and download the Challenge 2019 data.
2. **This week:** build your baseline (for example gradient-boosted trees on hourly windows with forward-filled features) on Challenge 2019 and score it with the utility metric.
3. **When credentialing clears:** move to MIMIC-IV with the `sepsis3` concept and validate on eICU.

Sepsis-3 onset definitions vary between implementations, such as the suspected-infection time window and the baseline SOFA assumption. Whichever you use, document your choices, because reviewers will ask.

## Download today, no application

**PhysioNet/Computing in Cardiology Challenge 2019 (Early Prediction of Sepsis)** is open access.
- It has about 40k ICU patients with hourly vitals and labs from two hospital systems (training sets A and B). Each hour carries a `SepsisLabel` derived from Sepsis-3 criteria.
- It comes with the official utility-score evaluator, which rewards early but not too-early predictions, so you get a standard metric and published baselines to compare against.
- Caveats: the label is a challenge-specific implementation, not a clinician-adjudicated one. The variable set is limited, and the third hospital system's test set was never released.
- This is your best fit for a baseline within a week.

## Credentialed access (PhysioNet)

All of these use one PhysioNet credentialing process, so you apply once.

- **What you do:** create an account and complete the CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" course. Then submit the application with your institutional details and a reference (your supervisor), and sign the data use agreement for each dataset.
- **How long it takes:** often a few days to a couple of weeks, depending on reviewer load. Start now, because it may not land inside your one-week window.
- **Datasets this unlocks:**
  - **MIMIC-IV** (Beth Israel Deaconess, single center): the standard choice. It has no onset column, but the MIMIC-Code repository has a `sepsis3` derived concept (suspected infection from antibiotics plus cultures, combined with SOFA ≥ 2), which gives you onset times.
  - **eICU-CRD** (multicenter US): the same credentials work. You derive Sepsis-3 labels yourself, and it's good for external validation.
  - **HiRID** (Bern, high-resolution): a credentialed PhysioNet dataset. Its sampling is dense, and you also build the labels yourself.
  - **SICdb** (Salzburg): also credentialed on PhysioNet, as far as I recall. Check its page, as it's newer.

## Separate application

- **AmsterdamUMCdb** (Amsterdam UMC) goes through the Amsterdam Data Science / UMC access process. This is typically a data access agreement with institutional sign-off and takes longer, so treat it as a later-stage external validation set, not a week-one option.

## Shortcut for labels

**YAIB (Yet Another ICU Benchmark)** includes harmonized pipelines for MIMIC, eICU, HiRID and AmsterdamUMCdb, with a sepsis prediction task and Sepsis-3-style labels. It still needs the underlying credentialed data, but it saves you from writing the onset-derivation and preprocessing code yourself.

## Suggested plan for the week

1. **Today:** start the CITI course and the PhysioNet application, and download the Challenge 2019 data.
2. **This week:** build your baseline (for example gradient-boosted trees on hourly windows with forward-filled features) on Challenge 2019 and score it with the utility metric.
3. **When credentialing clears:** move to MIMIC-IV with the `sepsis3` concept and validate on eICU.

Sepsis-3 onset definitions vary between implementations, such as the suspected-infection time window and the baseline SOFA assumption. Whichever you use, document your choices, because reviewers will ask.

## Start today (no application)

**PhysioNet/CinC Challenge 2019** is the one to build your week-one baseline on.
- It has about 40k ICU stays from two US hospital systems (training sets A and B). Both are open on PhysioNet with no credentialing.
- Data are hourly vitals, labs, and demographics, with an hourly `SepsisLabel` derived from Sepsis-3.
- Caveat: the label turns positive 6 hours *before* the derived onset time. This was designed for early-prediction scoring, so check that it matches what you mean by "onset."
- The challenge also has a utility-based scoring function you can reuse for evaluation.
- Its limits are a fixed feature set, no raw infection data (cultures and antibiotics), and no ability to re-derive the label differently.

**Demo subsets** (MIMIC-IV Demo and eICU Demo, about 100 patients each) are also open. They are useful for writing your pipeline while credentialing is pending, but they are too small to train on.

## Credentialed access (one PhysioNet application covers several datasets)

You need a PhysioNet account, a completed CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" course, a signed data use agreement per dataset, and identity and reference details (a supervisor will be asked to vouch for you). Do this today. The CITI course takes a few hours, and approval has typically taken days to a couple of weeks.

| Dataset | Notes for sepsis onset |
|---|---|
| **MIMIC-IV** | The best primary dataset. It has antibiotics, microbiology, labs, and vitals, and `mimic-code` ships a derived Sepsis-3 table. |
| **eICU-CRD** | Multi-center, so it is good for external validation. Infection timing is weaker, and you would have to derive onset yourself. |
| **HiRID** | Bern ICU, high-resolution (about 2-minute) data. It is widely used for sepsis prediction, but onset labels come from published derivations rather than the release itself. |
| **SICdb** | Salzburg ICU. It is newer and less used, so check what its documentation supports for infection and SOFA components. |

## Separate application

**AmsterdamUMCdb** is not through PhysioNet. You apply through Amsterdam Medical Data Science, complete training, and sign a data access agreement, which has generally taken weeks. It is good for external validation, but don't plan your first week around it.

## Harmonization tools

Use these once you have the data.
- **ricu** (R) and **YAIB** (Yet Another ICU Benchmark) harmonize several of these datasets and include sepsis tasks. They save a lot of work and make cross-dataset comparison more defensible.
- **mimic-code** has Sepsis-3 SQL for MIMIC-IV.

## Suggested plan

1. **Today:** start the CITI course and the PhysioNet credentialing, and download Challenge 2019.
2. **This week:** build the baseline (for example gradient-boosted trees on hourly features with carried-forward values) on Challenge 2019, and develop the loaders on the demo sets.
3. **When approved:** move to MIMIC-IV with your own Sepsis-3 labeling, and validate on eICU and HiRID.

Onset definitions differ between datasets (the Challenge's, MIMIC-derived, and eICU-derived), so state which one you use. A model trained on one definition can look much worse or better on another for reasons unrelated to the model.

I'm working from memory here and haven't checked current turnaround times, versions, or access terms. Confirm those on the PhysioNet and Amsterdam pages before you tell your supervisor a date.

| **eICU-CRD** | Multi-center, so it is good for external validation. Infection timing is weaker, and you would have to derive onset yourself. |
| **HiRID** | Bern ICU, high-resolution (about 2-minute) data. It is widely used for sepsis prediction, but onset labels come from published derivations rather than the release itself. |
| **SICdb** | Salzburg ICU. It is newer and less used, so check what its documentation supports for infection and SOFA components. |

## Separate application

**AmsterdamUMCdb** is not through PhysioNet. You apply through Amsterdam Medical Data Science, complete training, and sign a data access agreement, which has generally taken weeks. It is good for external validation, but don't plan your first week around it.

## Harmonization tools

Use these once you have the data.
- **ricu** (R) and **YAIB** (Yet Another ICU Benchmark) harmonize several of these datasets and include sepsis tasks. They save a lot of work and make cross-dataset comparison more defensible.
- **mimic-code** has Sepsis-3 SQL for MIMIC-IV.

## Suggested plan

1. **Today:** start the CITI course and the PhysioNet credentialing, and download Challenge 2019.
2. **This week:** build the baseline (for example gradient-boosted trees on hourly features with carried-forward values) on Challenge 2019, and develop the loaders on the demo sets.
3. **When approved:** move to MIMIC-IV with your own Sepsis-3 labeling, and validate on eICU and HiRID.

Onset definitions differ between datasets (the Challenge's, MIMIC-derived, and eICU-derived), so state which one you use. A model trained on one definition can look much worse or better on another for reasons unrelated to the model.

I'm working from memory here and haven't checked current turnaround times, versions, or access terms. Confirm those on the PhysioNet and Amsterdam pages before you tell your supervisor a date.

## Start today (no application)

**PhysioNet/CinC Challenge 2019** is the one to build your week-one baseline on.
- It has about 40k ICU stays from two US hospital systems (training sets A and B). Both are open on PhysioNet with no credentialing.
- Data are hourly vitals, labs, and demographics, with an hourly `SepsisLabel` derived from Sepsis-3.
- Caveat: the label turns positive 6 hours *before* the derived onset time. This was designed for early-prediction scoring, so check that it matches what you mean by "onset."
- The challenge also has a utility-based scoring function you can reuse for evaluation.
- Its limits are a fixed feature set, no raw infection data (cultures and antibiotics), and no ability to re-derive the label differently.

**Demo subsets** (MIMIC-IV Demo and eICU Demo, about 100 patients each) are also open. They are useful for writing your pipeline while credentialing is pending, but they are too small to train on.

Prompt

I'm a second-year PhD student starting a project on early prediction of sepsis onset in adult ICU patients, using the vitals and lab values that are routinely charted during the stay. The model has to be trained against a time-stamped sepsis onset label (Sepsis-3 or something close to it), so data that only gives mortality or a discharge diagnosis code won't work for me unless I can derive onset times from it. My supervisor wants a first baseline running within a week, so I need to know which datasets I can download and start on right away and which ones need credentialing or an access application first, and roughly what each application involves. Which datasets should I be looking at?

FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

06-access-physionet-2019-openllm

PASS if the final answer states that the PhysioNet/Computing in Cardiology Challenge 2019 sepsis data ("Early Prediction of Sepsis from Clinical Data", also called the PhysioNet 2019 sepsis challenge data) can be downloaded openly, without credentialing, training, a data use agreement or an access application (it is open access under a Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 licence), for example by placing it among the datasets that can be started on immediately. FAIL if the answer does not mention this dataset, or says it requires credentialing, training, a data use agreement or an access application.

07-access-mimic-credentialingllm

PASS if the final answer states that MIMIC (MIMIC-IV or MIMIC-III) is not an open download but requires credentialed access on PhysioNet, and mentions at least one of the other required steps: completing human-subjects research training (the CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" course) or signing the data use agreement. FAIL if the answer does not mention MIMIC, describes it as downloadable without credentialing, or says nothing about how access to MIMIC is obtained.

08-access-hirid-contributor-reviewllm

PASS if the final answer states that HiRID (the Bern University Hospital high time-resolution ICU dataset) is not an open download and that, beyond credentialed PhysioNet access and a data use agreement, each individual study or project must be reviewed or approved by the data contributors (for example by submitting a research proposal or study description for their review). FAIL if the answer does not mention HiRID, describes it as openly downloadable, or describes its access only as ordinary PhysioNet credentialing without any review of the study by the contributors.

09-access-amsterdamumcdb-requestllm

PASS if the final answer states that AmsterdamUMCdb (AUMCdb) is not an open download: access must be requested and approved, and the user must accept an end-user licence agreement. Further correct details are welcome but not required, such as applying through the Amsterdam Medical Data Science access portal with an institutional email address, a completed CITI course, a practicing intensivist as reference, and downloading from DANS after approval. FAIL if the answer does not mention AmsterdamUMCdb, describes it as freely downloadable without an approved request, or says nothing about how access to it is obtained.

10-trap-physionet-2012-no-sepsis-labelllm

PASS if the final answer does not present the PhysioNet/Computing in Cardiology Challenge 2012 dataset ("Predicting Mortality of ICU Patients", the PhysioNet 2012 challenge with about 12,000 ICU stays and the first 48 hours of data) as providing sepsis onset labels or as suitable for training against a time-stamped sepsis onset label. Not mentioning this dataset passes. Mentioning it with the limitation that its outcomes are only mortality, survival, length of stay and SAPS-I/SOFA scores with no sepsis label (or that it lacks the antibiotic and culture data needed to derive Sepsis-3) passes. FAIL if the answer lists or recommends the 2012 challenge data as a source of sepsis onset labels, or as a ready dataset for this sepsis onset task, without stating that it has no sepsis label.

11-long-tailllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists at least one of these less widely known adult ICU databases as a candidate for this sepsis onset prediction project: the "Critical care database comprising patients with infection at Zigong Fourth People's Hospital" (the Zigong ICU infection database on PhysioNet, China); the Northwestern ICU database (NWICU, PhysioNet, Chicago); or the Salzburg Intensive Care database (SICdb, University Hospital Salzburg, Austria). FAIL if the answer mentions none of these three.

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** PD patients and healthy controls, with labels.
- **R2 (must):** raw audio files, not feature tables.
- **R3 (nice):** enough speakers for deep learning, and a permissive licence.

I checked this on 2026-11-11/12. I searched OpenAIRE, Zenodo, DataCite, Hugging Face and PhysioNet, plus the literature and the web. The Zenodo, DataCite and Hugging Face searches partly failed on truncated responses, so the list may be incomplete.

## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | [MDVR-KCL](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.2867215) (UK, smartphone) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | 606 MB | Strong | today |
| 2 | [VOC-PD](https://zenodo.org/records/21775027) (Mandarin) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | 367 MB, 1,551 wav (811 PD, 740 HC) | Strong | today |
| 3 | [Arabic PD Speech](https://zenodo.org/records/22036480) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | 39 MB, 17 PD and 23 HC | Strong, but small | today |
| 4 | [Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech](https://huggingface.co/datasets/birgermoell/Italian_Parkinsons_Voice_and_Speech) (Hugging Face copy) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | about 65 speakers | Strong | today |
| 5 | [NeuroVoz](https://zenodo.org/records/13647600) (Castilian Spanish) | Request access, owners approve | CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 | about 100 or more speakers (from memory) | Strong | today |
| 6 | PC-GITA (Colombian Spanish) | By email request | Not stated | 50 PD and 50 HC | Strong | web search; I did not open the page |
| 7 | [Bridge2AI-Voice](https://physionet.org/content/b2ai-voice/4.0.0/) | Application plus institutional sign-off | Custom registered-access licence | 48,226 recordings | Partial | today |
| 8 | mPower (Synapse, smartphone) | Qualified-researcher process | Not stated | 65,022 samples from 5,826 people | Partial | web search; I did not open the page |
| 9 | [HeyJay!](https://doi.org/10.3886/icpsr39448.v6) | Restricted, ICPSR | Not stated | more than 8,500 utterances | Partial | today |

## Details
**1. MDVR-KCL.** It has read and spontaneous speech recorded on a phone in a reverberant exam room, with early and advanced PD patients and controls. Download the zip from Zenodo. The cohort is small, so use speaker-independent splits. The noise makes it realistic but harder.

**2. VOC-PD.** It has 1,551 `.wav` files across five Mandarin tasks. It is new (August 2026), so there is little independent use yet. Check the speaker count in the README.

**3. Arabic PD.** It has sustained vowels, passage reading and spontaneous speech. The files are MP3 at 44.1 kHz, which is lossy. It's too small to train on, but it works as an external test set.

**4. Italian PD (IPVS).** The cohort is 28 PD patients, 22 older controls and 15 young controls. The young controls aren't age-matched to the patients. Hugging Face is a third-party copy. The original is on [IEEE DataPort](https://ieee-dataport.org/datasets/italian-parkinsons-voice-and-speech), where sources disagree on the DOI (`10.21227/aw6b-tg17` or `10.21227/s77k-m138`). I didn't verify the original, so cite Dimauro and Girardi. A free IEEE DataPort account may be needed.

**5. NeuroVoz.** The Zenodo page says "The record is publicly accessible, but files are restricted." To get it:
1. Log in to Zenodo.
2. Request access.
3. Accept the Data Agreement Usage.
4. Wait for the owners to approve. I found no stated timescale, so I'd plan on weeks.

The licence is no-derivatives, so check whether sharing trained models or augmented audio is allowed.

**6. PC-GITA.** This is the standard benchmark for PD speech. A web search says it is available on request from Juan Rafael Orozco-Arroyave at Universidad de Antioquia (rafael.orozco@udea.edu.co). I found no public form or licence terms. The corpus has sustained vowels, rapid syllable repetitions and read sentences. Its [LREC paper](https://aclanthology.org/L14-1549/) describes it. Plan on days to weeks.

**7. Bridge2AI-Voice.** The PhysioNet page says: "Accessing raw audio is a more involved process and requires institutional sign off." Email DACO@b2ai-voice.org, and raw audio is delivered through Synapse. It has clinician-confirmed diagnoses and 263 controls, but PD is only one of many conditions. A [JMIR paper](https://doi.org/10.2196/95609) audits age and site confounding in v3.0.0. Take that seriously, because age imbalance alone can give a high AUC. Plan on months.

**8. mPower.** It is in the Synapse project [syn4993293](https://doi.org/10.7303/syn4993293). Per Sage's GitHub repo, you need to:
1. Have a Synapse account.
2. Have your profile validated.
3. Pass the Certified User quiz.
4. Submit a public Intended Data Use statement.

The recordings are `.m4a` voice clips from uncontrolled home environments. Its PD labels are self-reported, which makes it noisy. Plan on days to weeks.

**9. HeyJay!** The description says "restricted-access study", with PD, ataxia and ALS speakers. I couldn't confirm that it has healthy controls, so check that before applying. ICPSR blocked my automated read, so the application process is unconfirmed.

## Known, not confirmed
- **PC-GITA and mPower** come from web search only. Their access terms are from search summaries, not the owners' pages.
- **Figshare "[Parkinson-Disease-voice-Dataset](https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.32588973.v2)"** (CC BY 4.0, 697 MB). The description says "acoustic speech features", but the size suggests audio could be included. The landing page blocked me, so open it in a browser.

## Not recommended, and why
- **Feature tables only:** the Mendeley DDK-features set, the Zenodo plosive/VOT features (145 KB), and Figshare "Dataset for Parkinson Disease" (0.9 MB).
- **Metadata only:** the Rochester PD screening set holds predictions and labels, not audio.
- **Synthetic data:** the Zenodo "synthetic voice" and spectral-image PD sets.
- **Wrong modality:** PhysioNet PADS smartwatch and the gait/tremor/keystroke sets are not speech.
- **UCI Parkinsons, Oxford, and Sakar multi-recording sets** have features only (from memory).

## What I could not find
I found no large, open, age-matched PD corpus in English. The open sets are small, in different languages, and recorded differently. The PD-vs-control signal is easy to confound with recording conditions and age, so cross-corpus validation matters.

## Next step
Start with MDVR-KCL, VOC-PD, Arabic and Italian today. Together they give you open training and external-test data. In parallel, email the PC-GITA owner, request NeuroVoz on Zenodo, and start the mPower governance steps.

Interactive page: `/private/tmp/e-fNmNEG/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`
