## Shortlist
| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | [MDVR-KCL](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.2867215) (UK, smartphone) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | 606 MB | Strong | today |
| 2 | [VOC-PD](https://zenodo.org/records/21775027) (Mandarin) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | 367 MB, 1,551 wav (811 PD, 740 HC) | Strong | today |
| 3 | [Arabic PD Speech](https://zenodo.org/records/22036480) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | 39 MB, 17 PD and 23 HC | Strong, but small | today |
| 4 | [Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech](https://huggingface.co/datasets/birgermoell/Italian_Parkinsons_Voice_and_Speech) (Hugging Face copy) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | about 65 speakers | Strong | today |
| 5 | [NeuroVoz](https://zenodo.org/records/13647600) (Castilian Spanish) | Request access, owners approve | CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 | about 100 or more speakers (from memory) | Strong | today |
| 6 | PC-GITA (Colombian Spanish) | By email request | Not stated | 50 PD and 50 HC | Strong | web search; I did not open the page |
| 7 | [Bridge2AI-Voice](https://physionet.org/content/b2ai-voice/4.0.0/) | Application plus institutional sign-off | Custom registered-access licence | 48,226 recordings | Partial | today |
| 8 | mPower (Synapse, smartphone) | Qualified-researcher process | Not stated | 65,022 samples from 5,826 people | Partial | web search; I did not open the page |
| 9 | [HeyJay!](https://doi.org/10.3886/icpsr39448.v6) | Restricted, ICPSR | Not stated | more than 8,500 utterances | Partial | today |

## Details
**1. MDVR-KCL.** It has read and spontaneous speech recorded on a phone in a reverberant exam room, with early and advanced PD patients and controls. Download the zip from Zenodo. The cohort is small, so use speaker-independent splits. The noise makes it realistic but harder.

**2. VOC-PD.** It has 1,551 `.wav` files across five Mandarin tasks. It is new (August 2026), so there is little independent use yet. Check the speaker count in the README.

**3. Arabic PD.** It has sustained vowels, passage reading and spontaneous speech. The files are MP3 at 44.1 kHz, which is lossy. It's too small to train on, but it works as an external test set.

**4. Italian PD (IPVS).** The cohort is 28 PD patients, 22 older controls and 15 young controls. The young controls aren't age-matched to the patients. Hugging Face is a third-party copy. The original is on [IEEE DataPort](https://ieee-dataport.org/datasets/italian-parkinsons-voice-and-speech), where sources disagree on the DOI (`10.21227/aw6b-tg17` or `10.21227/s77k-m138`). I didn't verify the original, so cite Dimauro and Girardi. A free IEEE DataPort account may be needed.

**5. NeuroVoz.** The Zenodo page says "The record is publicly accessible, but files are restricted." To get it:
1. Log in to Zenodo.
2. Request access.
3. Accept the Data Agreement Usage.
4. Wait for the owners to approve. I found no stated timescale, so I'd plan on weeks.

The licence is no-derivatives, so check whether sharing trained models or augmented audio is allowed.

**6. PC-GITA.** This is the standard benchmark for PD speech. A web search says it is available on request from Juan Rafael Orozco-Arroyave at Universidad de Antioquia (rafael.orozco@udea.edu.co). I found no public form or licence terms. The corpus has sustained vowels, rapid syllable repetitions and read sentences. Its [LREC paper](https://aclanthology.org/L14-1549/) describes it. Plan on days to weeks.

**7. Bridge2AI-Voice.** The PhysioNet page says: "Accessing raw audio is a more involved process and requires institutional sign off." Email DACO@b2ai-voice.org, and raw audio is delivered through Synapse. It has clinician-confirmed diagnoses and 263 controls, but PD is only one of many conditions. A [JMIR paper](https://doi.org/10.2196/95609) audits age and site confounding in v3.0.0. Take that seriously, because age imbalance alone can give a high AUC. Plan on months.

**8. mPower.** It is in the Synapse project [syn4993293](https://doi.org/10.7303/syn4993293). Per Sage's GitHub repo, you need to:
1. Have a Synapse account.
2. Have your profile validated.
3. Pass the Certified User quiz.
4. Submit a public Intended Data Use statement.

The recordings are `.m4a` voice clips from uncontrolled home environments. Its PD labels are self-reported, which makes it noisy. Plan on days to weeks.

**9. HeyJay!** The description says "restricted-access study", with PD, ataxia and ALS speakers. I couldn't confirm that it has healthy controls, so check that before applying. ICPSR blocked my automated read, so the application process is unconfirmed.

## Known, not confirmed
- **PC-GITA and mPower** come from web search only. Their access terms are from search summaries, not the owners' pages.
- **Figshare "[Parkinson-Disease-voice-Dataset](https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.32588973.v2)"** (CC BY 4.0, 697 MB). The description says "acoustic speech features", but the size suggests audio could be included. The landing page blocked me, so open it in a browser.

## Not recommended, and why
- **Feature tables only:** the Mendeley DDK-features set, the Zenodo plosive/VOT features (145 KB), and Figshare "Dataset for Parkinson Disease" (0.9 MB).
- **Metadata only:** the Rochester PD screening set holds predictions and labels, not audio.
- **Synthetic data:** the Zenodo "synthetic voice" and spectral-image PD sets.
- **Wrong modality:** PhysioNet PADS smartwatch and the gait/tremor/keystroke sets are not speech.
- **UCI Parkinsons, Oxford, and Sakar multi-recording sets** have features only (from memory).

## What I could not find
I found no large, open, age-matched PD corpus in English. The open sets are small, in different languages, and recorded differently. The PD-vs-control signal is easy to confound with recording conditions and age, so cross-corpus validation matters.

## Next step
Start with MDVR-KCL, VOC-PD, Arabic and Italian today. Together they give you open training and external-test data. In parallel, email the PC-GITA owner, request NeuroVoz on Zenodo, and start the mPower governance steps.

Interactive page: `/private/tmp/e-fNmNEG/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | [MDVR-KCL](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.2867215) (UK, smartphone) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | 606 MB | Strong | today |
| 2 | [VOC-PD](https://zenodo.org/records/21775027) (Mandarin) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | 367 MB, 1,551 wav (811 PD, 740 HC) | Strong | today |
| 3 | [Arabic PD Speech](https://zenodo.org/records/22036480) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | 39 MB, 17 PD and 23 HC | Strong, but small | today |
| 4 | [Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech](https://huggingface.co/datasets/birgermoell/Italian_Parkinsons_Voice_and_Speech) (Hugging Face copy) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | about 65 speakers | Strong | today |
| 5 | [NeuroVoz](https://zenodo.org/records/13647600) (Castilian Spanish) | Request access, owners approve | CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 | about 100 or more speakers (from memory) | Strong | today |
| 6 | PC-GITA (Colombian Spanish) | By email request | Not stated | 50 PD and 50 HC | Strong | web search; I did not open the page |
| 7 | [Bridge2AI-Voice](https://physionet.org/content/b2ai-voice/4.0.0/) | Application plus institutional sign-off | Custom registered-access licence | 48,226 recordings | Partial | today |
| 8 | mPower (Synapse, smartphone) | Qualified-researcher process | Not stated | 65,022 samples from 5,826 people | Partial | web search; I did not open the page |
| 9 | [HeyJay!](https://doi.org/10.3886/icpsr39448.v6) | Restricted, ICPSR | Not stated | more than 8,500 utterances | Partial | today |

## Details
**1. MDVR-KCL.** It has read and spontaneous speech recorded on a phone in a reverberant exam room, with early and advanced PD patients and controls. Download the zip from Zenodo. The cohort is small, so use speaker-independent splits. The noise makes it realistic but harder.

**2. VOC-PD.** It has 1,551 `.wav` files across five Mandarin tasks. It is new (August 2026), so there is little independent use yet. Check the speaker count in the README.

**3. Arabic PD.** It has sustained vowels, passage reading and spontaneous speech. The files are MP3 at 44.1 kHz, which is lossy. It's too small to train on, but it works as an external test set.

**4. Italian PD (IPVS).** The cohort is 28 PD patients, 22 older controls and 15 young controls. The young controls aren't age-matched to the patients. Hugging Face is a third-party copy. The original is on [IEEE DataPort](https://ieee-dataport.org/datasets/italian-parkinsons-voice-and-speech), where sources disagree on the DOI (`10.21227/aw6b-tg17` or `10.21227/s77k-m138`). I didn't verify the original, so cite Dimauro and Girardi. A free IEEE DataPort account may be needed.

**5. NeuroVoz.** The Zenodo page says "The record is publicly accessible, but files are restricted." To get it:
1. Log in to Zenodo.
2. Request access.
3. Accept the Data Agreement Usage.
4. Wait for the owners to approve. I found no stated timescale, so I'd plan on weeks.

The licence is no-derivatives, so check whether sharing trained models or augmented audio is allowed.

**6. PC-GITA.** This is the standard benchmark for PD speech. A web search says it is available on request from Juan Rafael Orozco-Arroyave at Universidad de Antioquia (rafael.orozco@udea.edu.co). I found no public form or licence terms. The corpus has sustained vowels, rapid syllable repetitions and read sentences. Its [LREC paper](https://aclanthology.org/L14-1549/) describes it. Plan on days to weeks.

**7. Bridge2AI-Voice.** The PhysioNet page says: "Accessing raw audio is a more involved process and requires institutional sign off." Email DACO@b2ai-voice.org, and raw audio is delivered through Synapse. It has clinician-confirmed diagnoses and 263 controls, but PD is only one of many conditions. A [JMIR paper](https://doi.org/10.2196/95609) audits age and site confounding in v3.0.0. Take that seriously, because age imbalance alone can give a high AUC. Plan on months.

**8. mPower.** It is in the Synapse project [syn4993293](https://doi.org/10.7303/syn4993293). Per Sage's GitHub repo, you need to:
1. Have a Synapse account.
2. Have your profile validated.
3. Pass the Certified User quiz.
4. Submit a public Intended Data Use statement.

The recordings are `.m4a` voice clips from uncontrolled home environments. Its PD labels are self-reported, which makes it noisy. Plan on days to weeks.

**9. HeyJay!** The description says "restricted-access study", with PD, ataxia and ALS speakers. I couldn't confirm that it has healthy controls, so check that before applying. ICPSR blocked my automated read, so the application process is unconfirmed.

## Known, not confirmed
- **PC-GITA and mPower** come from web search only. Their access terms are from search summaries, not the owners' pages.
- **Figshare "[Parkinson-Disease-voice-Dataset](https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.32588973.v2)"** (CC BY 4.0, 697 MB). The description says "acoustic speech features", but the size suggests audio could be included. The landing page blocked me, so open it in a browser.

## Not recommended, and why
- **Feature tables only:** the Mendeley DDK-features set, the Zenodo plosive/VOT features (145 KB), and Figshare "Dataset for Parkinson Disease" (0.9 MB).
- **Metadata only:** the Rochester PD screening set holds predictions and labels, not audio.
- **Synthetic data:** the Zenodo "synthetic voice" and spectral-image PD sets.
- **Wrong modality:** PhysioNet PADS smartwatch and the gait/tremor/keystroke sets are not speech.
- **UCI Parkinsons, Oxford, and Sakar multi-recording sets** have features only (from memory).

## What I could not find
I found no large, open, age-matched PD corpus in English. The open sets are small, in different languages, and recorded differently. The PD-vs-control signal is easy to confound with recording conditions and age, so cross-corpus validation matters.

## Next step
Start with MDVR-KCL, VOC-PD, Arabic and Italian today. Together they give you open training and external-test data. In parallel, email the PC-GITA owner, request NeuroVoz on Zenodo, and start the mPower governance steps.

Interactive page: `/private/tmp/e-fNmNEG/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** real audio files, not feature tables.
- **R2 (must):** labelled PD and healthy-control speakers.
- **R3 (nice):** a licence that allows model training.
- **R4 (nice):** enough speakers for deep learning, ideally 100 or more.
- I assumed you can wait weeks for an application if the data is worth it, and that the language doesn't matter.

I checked on 2026-11-12. "Confirmed" means a registry record or page I read this session. "Memory" or "web search" means not confirmed on the source itself.

## Shortlist

| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | MDVR-KCL (smartphone, English) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | About 37 speakers (memory) | Strong, small | Confirmed |
| 2 | VOC-PD (Mandarin) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | 1,551 WAV files, 811 PD and 740 HC | Strong | Registry record confirmed; page not fully read |
| 3 | Arabic PD Speech | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | 17 PD, 23 HC (MP3) | Strong, small | Confirmed |
| 4 | Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech | Hugging Face mirror open; original needs a free IEEE account (memory) | CC BY 4.0 on the mirror | 28 PD, 37 HC | Strong | Mirror confirmed; original from web search |
| 5 | NeuroVoz (Castilian Spanish) | Request access on Zenodo, about weeks | CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 | 54 PD, 58 HC | Strong, the best size | Confirmed |
| 6 | PC-GITA (Colombian Spanish) | Email request and signed agreement | Agreement terms | 50 PD, 50 HC | Strong | Web search only |
| 7 | mPower (smartphone voice) | Synapse qualified-researcher access | Synapse terms | Thousands of participants | Partial | Web search only |
| 8 | Bridge2AI-Voice | PhysioNet credentialing, plus committee sign-off for raw audio | Bridge2AI Registered Access Licence | 253 participants in one PD analysis | Partial | Confirmed |
| 9 | HeyJay! (ICPSR 39448) | Restricted data use agreement, about 3 weeks | Not confirmed | Not confirmed | Weak | Web search only |

## Details

**1. MDVR-KCL** ([record](https://zenodo.org/records/2867216))
- It holds 44.1 kHz, 16-bit WAV files from early and advanced PD patients and healthy controls.
- File names encode the label and clinical scores (for example `ID02_pd_1_2_1.wav`).
- Access: open the record and download. No account is needed.

**2. VOC-PD** ([DOI](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21775027))
- It has 1,551 WAV recordings (numbers, phrases, sentences, vowels), about 367 MB in 4 zip files.
- The Zenodo page timed out when I read it, so the counts come from the registry description.
- Check whether the recordings carry speaker IDs, so you can split train and test by speaker.

**3. Arabic PD Speech** ([record](https://zenodo.org/records/22036480))
- It has vowels, a read passage and spontaneous speech, as 44.1 kHz MP3 (lossy), about 39 MB.
- Download directly. The page states CC BY 4.0.

**4. Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech**
- The original is by Dimauro and Girardi on IEEE DataPort, DOI 10.21227/aw6b-tg17 ([search result](https://commons.datacite.org/doi.org/10.21227/kv93-ak67)).
- It has 28 PD patients, 15 young healthy controls and 22 elderly healthy controls. Many papers use only the 22 elderly controls to avoid an age confound.
- A copy sits on [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/datasets/birgermoell/Italian_Parkinsons_Voice_and_Speech) (1,662 clips, CC BY 4.0). Its card doesn't show the PD/HC labels, so check them before training.
- Cite the original authors, not the mirror.

**5. NeuroVoz** ([record](https://zenodo.org/records/13647600))
- It has 112 participants, with PD patients recorded on medication.
- Access: log in to Zenodo, request access, and accept the Data Agreement Usage. The authors approve requests.
- The page shows **CC BY-NC-ND 4.0**. The no-derivatives clause may matter for model training, so read the agreement.

**6. PC-GITA** (web search, not a registry record)
- It has 50 PD and 50 HC speakers with vowels, DDK tasks, sentences and monologues. An extended version adds 20 PD and 20 HC recorded in uncontrolled conditions.
- Access: request it from the GITA Lab, University of Antioquia (rafael.orozco@udea.edu.co) and sign a user agreement. The standard citation is Orozco-Arroyave et al., LREC 2014.
- It is the most widely used PD speech benchmark, so it is useful for comparing results.

**7. mPower** (web search; Synapse ID syn4993293)
- It has real-world smartphone voice recordings (sustained /a/, m4a) from a very large app cohort.
- Access: a Synapse account validated by the Access and Compliance Team, a Certified User quiz, and a public Intended Data Use statement. Only participants who opted into broad sharing are included.
- Labels are self-reported, and the "controls" are app users who didn't report PD. That is noisy for detection work.

**8. Bridge2AI-Voice** ([PhysioNet](https://physionet.org/content/b2ai-voice/4.0.0/))
- The PhysioNet release contains no raw audio. Raw audio needs institutional sign-off from the access committee (DACO@b2ai-voice.org, per a web search result).
- One published analysis built a cohort of 106 PD cases and 147 controls from it.

**9. HeyJay!** ([ICPSR](https://www.icpsr.umich.edu/web/ICPSR/studies/39448/versions/V6))
- It is a corpus of atypical English speech that includes PD speakers, built for speech recognition rather than detection.
- I could not confirm that it has healthy controls. Only consider it if you can confirm a control group.

## Known, not confirmed
- **PC-GITA, mPower and the original Italian record** have no registry record. Their access terms come from web search and the Italian licence from the mirror, so confirm each at the source before planning around it.
- **Hugging Face MDVR-KCL copy** (`saeedzou/kcl-parkinson-voice-dataset`): it is gated behind a click-through. Use the Zenodo original instead.
- **Dataset-in-paper "ParkMultiNet"** ([Zenodo](https://zenodo.org/records/22082939)): it is open (CC BY 4.0) with 502 processed speech clips (266 PD, 236 non-PD). The clips are re-processed from other public sets, so using it risks duplicating data you already have.

## Not recommended, and why
- **Figshare "Parkinson-Disease-voice-Dataset", Mendeley DDK set, Zenodo plosive set, Hugging Face `TabBioMed` and `BenchmarkDatasets` Parkinson_Speech:** these hold extracted features or tables, so they fail R1.
- **PADS smartwatch, gait, tremor and keystroke sets on PhysioNet:** they have no speech audio.

## What I could not find
- **Large open datasets:** no open dataset with 100 or more speakers turned up. The largest open sets are the Mandarin one (speaker count unknown) and the Italian one (65 speakers).
- **Registry coverage:** DataCite and Hugging Face dropped out on some queries. The figshare and ICPSR pages blocked automated reading.
- **Reuse caveat:** most sets use different languages and recording setups. A model trained on one often drops sharply on another, so plan for cross-corpus testing.

## Next step
Download MDVR-KCL, VOC-PD, Arabic and Italian today. Send the NeuroVoz request and the PC-GITA email now, since both take time. Together they give you several languages for cross-corpus testing.

Interactive page: `/private/tmp/e-LXXiUX/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** real audio files, not feature tables.
- **R2 (must):** labelled PD and healthy-control speakers.
- **R3 (nice):** a licence that allows model training.
- **R4 (nice):** enough speakers for deep learning, ideally 100 or more.
- I assumed you can wait weeks for an application if the data is worth it, and that the language doesn't matter.

I checked on 2026-11-12. "Confirmed" means a registry record or page I read this session. "Memory" or "web search" means not confirmed on the source itself.

- **Large open datasets:** no open dataset with 100 or more speakers turned up. The largest open sets are the Mandarin one (speaker count unknown) and the Italian one (65 speakers).
- **Registry coverage:** DataCite and Hugging Face dropped out on some queries. The figshare and ICPSR pages blocked automated reading.
- **Reuse caveat:** most sets use different languages and recording setups. A model trained on one often drops sharply on another, so plan for cross-corpus testing.

## Next step
Download MDVR-KCL, VOC-PD, Arabic and Italian today. Send the NeuroVoz request and the PC-GITA email now, since both take time. Together they give you several languages for cross-corpus testing.

✓ pass 05-access-mdvr-kcl-open

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** real audio files, not feature tables.
- **R2 (must):** labelled PD and healthy-control speakers.
- **R3 (nice):** a licence that allows model training.
- **R4 (nice):** enough speakers for deep learning, ideally 100 or more.
- I assumed you can wait weeks for an application if the data is worth it, and that the language doesn't matter.

I checked on 2026-11-12. "Confirmed" means a registry record or page I read this session. "Memory" or "web search" means not confirmed on the source itself.

## Shortlist

| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | MDVR-KCL (smartphone, English) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | About 37 speakers (memory) | Strong, small | Confirmed |
| 2 | VOC-PD (Mandarin) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | 1,551 WAV files, 811 PD and 740 HC | Strong | Registry record confirmed; page not fully read |
| 3 | Arabic PD Speech | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | 17 PD, 23 HC (MP3) | Strong, small | Confirmed |
| 4 | Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech | Hugging Face mirror open; original needs a free IEEE account (memory) | CC BY 4.0 on the mirror | 28 PD, 37 HC | Strong | Mirror confirmed; original from web search |
| 5 | NeuroVoz (Castilian Spanish) | Request access on Zenodo, about weeks | CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 | 54 PD, 58 HC | Strong, the best size | Confirmed |
| 6 | PC-GITA (Colombian Spanish) | Email request and signed agreement | Agreement terms | 50 PD, 50 HC | Strong | Web search only |
| 7 | mPower (smartphone voice) | Synapse qualified-researcher access | Synapse terms | Thousands of participants | Partial | Web search only |
| 8 | Bridge2AI-Voice | PhysioNet credentialing, plus committee sign-off for raw audio | Bridge2AI Registered Access Licence | 253 participants in one PD analysis | Partial | Confirmed |
| 9 | HeyJay! (ICPSR 39448) | Restricted data use agreement, about 3 weeks | Not confirmed | Not confirmed | Weak | Web search only |

## Details

**1. MDVR-KCL** ([record](https://zenodo.org/records/2867216))
- It holds 44.1 kHz, 16-bit WAV files from early and advanced PD patients and healthy controls.
- File names encode the label and clinical scores (for example `ID02_pd_1_2_1.wav`).
- Access: open the record and download. No account is needed.

**2. VOC-PD** ([DOI](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21775027))
- It has 1,551 WAV recordings (numbers, phrases, sentences, vowels), about 367 MB in 4 zip files.
- The Zenodo page timed out when I read it, so the counts come from the registry description.
- Check whether the recordings carry speaker IDs, so you can split train and test by speaker.

**3. Arabic PD Speech** ([record](https://zenodo.org/records/22036480))
- It has vowels, a read passage and spontaneous speech, as 44.1 kHz MP3 (lossy), about 39 MB.
- Download directly. The page states CC BY 4.0.

**4. Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech**
- The original is by Dimauro and Girardi on IEEE DataPort, DOI 10.21227/aw6b-tg17 ([search result](https://commons.datacite.org/doi.org/10.21227/kv93-ak67)).
- It has 28 PD patients, 15 young healthy controls and 22 elderly healthy controls. Many papers use only the 22 elderly controls to avoid an age confound.
- A copy sits on [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/datasets/birgermoell/Italian_Parkinsons_Voice_and_Speech) (1,662 clips, CC BY 4.0). Its card doesn't show the PD/HC labels, so check them before training.
- Cite the original authors, not the mirror.

**5. NeuroVoz** ([record](https://zenodo.org/records/13647600))
- It has 112 participants, with PD patients recorded on medication.
- Access: log in to Zenodo, request access, and accept the Data Agreement Usage. The authors approve requests.
- The page shows **CC BY-NC-ND 4.0**. The no-derivatives clause may matter for model training, so read the agreement.

**6. PC-GITA** (web search, not a registry record)
- It has 50 PD and 50 HC speakers with vowels, DDK tasks, sentences and monologues. An extended version adds 20 PD and 20 HC recorded in uncontrolled conditions.
- Access: request it from the GITA Lab, University of Antioquia (rafael.orozco@udea.edu.co) and sign a user agreement. The standard citation is Orozco-Arroyave et al., LREC 2014.
- It is the most widely used PD speech benchmark, so it is useful for comparing results.

**7. mPower** (web search; Synapse ID syn4993293)
- It has real-world smartphone voice recordings (sustained /a/, m4a) from a very large app cohort.
- Access: a Synapse account validated by the Access and Compliance Team, a Certified User quiz, and a public Intended Data Use statement. Only participants who opted into broad sharing are included.
- Labels are self-reported, and the "controls" are app users who didn't report PD. That is noisy for detection work.

**8. Bridge2AI-Voice** ([PhysioNet](https://physionet.org/content/b2ai-voice/4.0.0/))
- The PhysioNet release contains no raw audio. Raw audio needs institutional sign-off from the access committee (DACO@b2ai-voice.org, per a web search result).
- One published analysis built a cohort of 106 PD cases and 147 controls from it.

**9. HeyJay!** ([ICPSR](https://www.icpsr.umich.edu/web/ICPSR/studies/39448/versions/V6))
- It is a corpus of atypical English speech that includes PD speakers, built for speech recognition rather than detection.
- I could not confirm that it has healthy controls. Only consider it if you can confirm a control group.

## Known, not confirmed
- **PC-GITA, mPower and the original Italian record** have no registry record. Their access terms come from web search and the Italian licence from the mirror, so confirm each at the source before planning around it.
- **Hugging Face MDVR-KCL copy** (`saeedzou/kcl-parkinson-voice-dataset`): it is gated behind a click-through. Use the Zenodo original instead.
- **Dataset-in-paper "ParkMultiNet"** ([Zenodo](https://zenodo.org/records/22082939)): it is open (CC BY 4.0) with 502 processed speech clips (266 PD, 236 non-PD). The clips are re-processed from other public sets, so using it risks duplicating data you already have.

## Not recommended, and why
- **Figshare "Parkinson-Disease-voice-Dataset", Mendeley DDK set, Zenodo plosive set, Hugging Face `TabBioMed` and `BenchmarkDatasets` Parkinson_Speech:** these hold extracted features or tables, so they fail R1.
- **PADS smartwatch, gait, tremor and keystroke sets on PhysioNet:** they have no speech audio.

## What I could not find
- **Large open datasets:** no open dataset with 100 or more speakers turned up. The largest open sets are the Mandarin one (speaker count unknown) and the Italian one (65 speakers).
- **Registry coverage:** DataCite and Hugging Face dropped out on some queries. The figshare and ICPSR pages blocked automated reading.
- **Reuse caveat:** most sets use different languages and recording setups. A model trained on one often drops sharply on another, so plan for cross-corpus testing.

## Next step
Download MDVR-KCL, VOC-PD, Arabic and Italian today. Send the NeuroVoz request and the PC-GITA email now, since both take time. Together they give you several languages for cross-corpus testing.

- The PhysioNet release contains no raw audio. Raw audio needs institutional sign-off from the access committee (DACO@b2ai-voice.org, per a web search result).
- One published analysis built a cohort of 106 PD cases and 147 controls from it.

**9. HeyJay!** ([ICPSR](https://www.icpsr.umich.edu/web/ICPSR/studies/39448/versions/V6))
- It is a corpus of atypical English speech that includes PD speakers, built for speech recognition rather than detection.
- I could not confirm that it has healthy controls. Only consider it if you can confirm a control group.

## Known, not confirmed
- **PC-GITA, mPower and the original Italian record** have no registry record. Their access terms come from web search and the Italian licence from the mirror, so confirm each at the source before planning around it.
- **Hugging Face MDVR-KCL copy** (`saeedzou/kcl-parkinson-voice-dataset`): it is gated behind a click-through. Use the Zenodo original instead.
- **Dataset-in-paper "ParkMultiNet"** ([Zenodo](https://zenodo.org/records/22082939)): it is open (CC BY 4.0) with 502 processed speech clips (266 PD, 236 non-PD). The clips are re-processed from other public sets, so using it risks duplicating data you already have.

## Not recommended, and why
- **Figshare "Parkinson-Disease-voice-Dataset", Mendeley DDK set, Zenodo plosive set, Hugging Face `TabBioMed` and `BenchmarkDatasets` Parkinson_Speech:** these hold extracted features or tables, so they fail R1.
- **PADS smartwatch, gait, tremor and keystroke sets on PhysioNet:** they have no speech audio.

## What I could not find
- **Large open datasets:** no open dataset with 100 or more speakers turned up. The largest open sets are the Mandarin one (speaker count unknown) and the Italian one (65 speakers).
- **Registry coverage:** DataCite and Hugging Face dropped out on some queries. The figshare and ICPSR pages blocked automated reading.
- **Reuse caveat:** most sets use different languages and recording setups. A model trained on one often drops sharply on another, so plan for cross-corpus testing.

## Next step
Download MDVR-KCL, VOC-PD, Arabic and Italian today. Send the NeuroVoz request and the PC-GITA email now, since both take time. Together they give you several languages for cross-corpus testing.

## What I assumed you need
- **R1 (must):** real audio files, not feature tables.
- **R2 (must):** labelled PD and healthy-control speakers.
- **R3 (nice):** a licence that allows model training.
- **R4 (nice):** enough speakers for deep learning, ideally 100 or more.
- I assumed you can wait weeks for an application if the data is worth it, and that the language doesn't matter.

I checked on 2026-11-12. "Confirmed" means a registry record or page I read this session. "Memory" or "web search" means not confirmed on the source itself.

## Shortlist

| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit | Checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | MDVR-KCL (smartphone, English) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | About 37 speakers (memory) | Strong, small | Confirmed |
| 2 | VOC-PD (Mandarin) | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | 1,551 WAV files, 811 PD and 740 HC | Strong | Registry record confirmed; page not fully read |
| 3 | Arabic PD Speech | Open download | CC BY 4.0 | 17 PD, 23 HC (MP3) | Strong, small | Confirmed |
| 4 | Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech | Hugging Face mirror open; original needs a free IEEE account (memory) | CC BY 4.0 on the mirror | 28 PD, 37 HC | Strong | Mirror confirmed; original from web search |
| 5 | NeuroVoz (Castilian Spanish) | Request access on Zenodo, about weeks | CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 | 54 PD, 58 HC | Strong, the best size | Confirmed |
| 6 | PC-GITA (Colombian Spanish) | Email request and signed agreement | Agreement terms | 50 PD, 50 HC | Strong | Web search only |
| 7 | mPower (smartphone voice) | Synapse qualified-researcher access | Synapse terms | Thousands of participants | Partial | Web search only |
| 8 | Bridge2AI-Voice | PhysioNet credentialing, plus committee sign-off for raw audio | Bridge2AI Registered Access Licence | 253 participants in one PD analysis | Partial | Confirmed |
| 9 | HeyJay! (ICPSR 39448) | Restricted data use agreement, about 3 weeks | Not confirmed | Not confirmed | Weak | Web search only |

## Details

**1. MDVR-KCL** ([record](https://zenodo.org/records/2867216))
- It holds 44.1 kHz, 16-bit WAV files from early and advanced PD patients and healthy controls.
- File names encode the label and clinical scores (for example `ID02_pd_1_2_1.wav`).
- Access: open the record and download. No account is needed.

**2. VOC-PD** ([DOI](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21775027))
- It has 1,551 WAV recordings (numbers, phrases, sentences, vowels), about 367 MB in 4 zip files.
- The Zenodo page timed out when I read it, so the counts come from the registry description.
- Check whether the recordings carry speaker IDs, so you can split train and test by speaker.

**3. Arabic PD Speech** ([record](https://zenodo.org/records/22036480))
- It has vowels, a read passage and spontaneous speech, as 44.1 kHz MP3 (lossy), about 39 MB.
- Download directly. The page states CC BY 4.0.

**4. Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech**
- The original is by Dimauro and Girardi on IEEE DataPort, DOI 10.21227/aw6b-tg17 ([search result](https://commons.datacite.org/doi.org/10.21227/kv93-ak67)).
- It has 28 PD patients, 15 young healthy controls and 22 elderly healthy controls. Many papers use only the 22 elderly controls to avoid an age confound.
- A copy sits on [Hugging Face](https://huggingface.co/datasets/birgermoell/Italian_Parkinsons_Voice_and_Speech) (1,662 clips, CC BY 4.0). Its card doesn't show the PD/HC labels, so check them before training.
- Cite the original authors, not the mirror.

**5. NeuroVoz** ([record](https://zenodo.org/records/13647600))
- It has 112 participants, with PD patients recorded on medication.
- Access: log in to Zenodo, request access, and accept the Data Agreement Usage. The authors approve requests.
- The page shows **CC BY-NC-ND 4.0**. The no-derivatives clause may matter for model training, so read the agreement.

**6. PC-GITA** (web search, not a registry record)
- It has 50 PD and 50 HC speakers with vowels, DDK tasks, sentences and monologues. An extended version adds 20 PD and 20 HC recorded in uncontrolled conditions.
- Access: request it from the GITA Lab, University of Antioquia (rafael.orozco@udea.edu.co) and sign a user agreement. The standard citation is Orozco-Arroyave et al., LREC 2014.
- It is the most widely used PD speech benchmark, so it is useful for comparing results.

**7. mPower** (web search; Synapse ID syn4993293)
- It has real-world smartphone voice recordings (sustained /a/, m4a) from a very large app cohort.
- Access: a Synapse account validated by the Access and Compliance Team, a Certified User quiz, and a public Intended Data Use statement. Only participants who opted into broad sharing are included.
- Labels are self-reported, and the "controls" are app users who didn't report PD. That is noisy for detection work.

**8. Bridge2AI-Voice** ([PhysioNet](https://physionet.org/content/b2ai-voice/4.0.0/))
- The PhysioNet release contains no raw audio. Raw audio needs institutional sign-off from the access committee (DACO@b2ai-voice.org, per a web search result).
- One published analysis built a cohort of 106 PD cases and 147 controls from it.

**9. HeyJay!** ([ICPSR](https://www.icpsr.umich.edu/web/ICPSR/studies/39448/versions/V6))
- It is a corpus of atypical English speech that includes PD speakers, built for speech recognition rather than detection.
- I could not confirm that it has healthy controls. Only consider it if you can confirm a control group.

## Known, not confirmed
- **PC-GITA, mPower and the original Italian record** have no registry record. Their access terms come from web search and the Italian licence from the mirror, so confirm each at the source before planning around it.
- **Hugging Face MDVR-KCL copy** (`saeedzou/kcl-parkinson-voice-dataset`): it is gated behind a click-through. Use the Zenodo original instead.
- **Dataset-in-paper "ParkMultiNet"** ([Zenodo](https://zenodo.org/records/22082939)): it is open (CC BY 4.0) with 502 processed speech clips (266 PD, 236 non-PD). The clips are re-processed from other public sets, so using it risks duplicating data you already have.

## Not recommended, and why
- **Figshare "Parkinson-Disease-voice-Dataset", Mendeley DDK set, Zenodo plosive set, Hugging Face `TabBioMed` and `BenchmarkDatasets` Parkinson_Speech:** these hold extracted features or tables, so they fail R1.
- **PADS smartwatch, gait, tremor and keystroke sets on PhysioNet:** they have no speech audio.

## What I could not find
- **Large open datasets:** no open dataset with 100 or more speakers turned up. The largest open sets are the Mandarin one (speaker count unknown) and the Italian one (65 speakers).
- **Registry coverage:** DataCite and Hugging Face dropped out on some queries. The figshare and ICPSR pages blocked automated reading.
- **Reuse caveat:** most sets use different languages and recording setups. A model trained on one often drops sharply on another, so plan for cross-corpus testing.

## Next step
Download MDVR-KCL, VOC-PD, Arabic and Italian today. Send the NeuroVoz request and the PC-GITA email now, since both take time. Together they give you several languages for cross-corpus testing.

Here's what I know from memory. I haven't checked any of these links live, so confirm current access terms on each landing page.

## Open or easy to get

| Dataset | Contents | Access |
|---|---|---|
| **Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech** (Dimauro & Girardi) | About 65 subjects: 28 PD, 15 elderly controls, 22 young controls. Sustained vowels, words and sentences as WAV files. | Free on IEEE DataPort. You need an account, and open access is available. It's also mirrored on Kaggle. |
| **MDVR-KCL** (King's College London) | 16 PD and 21 controls (the PD group includes people with REM sleep behavior disorder). Smartphone recordings of reading and spontaneous speech. | Open on Zenodo, CC BY 4.0. Search for "Mobile Device Voice Recordings at King's College London." |

## Free, but you must apply

| Dataset | Contents | Access |
|---|---|---|
| **mPower** (Sage Bionetworks) | Thousands of smartphone recordings of a 10-second "aaah", with self-reported PD and control labels. It's by far the largest, but the recordings are noisy and the labels are self-reported. | Synapse account, a short certification quiz, then a data use application. |
| **Neurovoz** (Spanish, Castilian) | About 50 PD and 50 controls, with vowels, DDK tasks and sentences. | Hosted on Zenodo with restricted access. You request it with a data use agreement. |
| **PC-GITA** (Colombian Spanish, GITA Lab, Universidad de Antioquia) | 50 PD and 50 age/sex-matched controls, with vowels, DDK, words, sentences and a monologue. It's the most widely cited benchmark. | Email the GITA group and sign a license agreement, usually for academic use. |

## Possible, but check first
- **Bridge2AI-Voice** (PhysioNet) includes a PD category. As I recall, the public release has features and spectrograms rather than raw audio because of privacy, so check whether it fits your need.
- **Czech and German PD speech corpora** (for example, from Rusz's group in Prague) exist in the literature. They're generally available only through collaboration.

## Common traps
- The **UCI Parkinson's datasets** (Little, Sakar, telemonitoring) have precomputed features only, so they don't meet your requirement. Most Kaggle "Parkinson's voice" datasets are copies of these.
- Most of these are small, with fewer than 100 subjects. For deep learning, split by speaker, never by recording. Otherwise the same speaker lands in both train and test, and your accuracy will be inflated.
- Recording conditions, language and device differ between datasets. A model can learn to identify the dataset or microphone instead of the disease, so avoid training on PD from one source and controls from another.
- Pretrained audio encoders (wav2vec 2.0, HuBERT, Whisper) fine-tuned or used as feature extractors tend to work better than training from scratch at this scale.

I can search the web to confirm the current links and access requirements for any of these. I can also help set up a speaker-independent split once you've picked a dataset.

| **Neurovoz** (Spanish, Castilian) | About 50 PD and 50 controls, with vowels, DDK tasks and sentences. | Hosted on Zenodo with restricted access. You request it with a data use agreement. |
| **PC-GITA** (Colombian Spanish, GITA Lab, Universidad de Antioquia) | 50 PD and 50 age/sex-matched controls, with vowels, DDK, words, sentences and a monologue. It's the most widely cited benchmark. | Email the GITA group and sign a license agreement, usually for academic use. |

## Possible, but check first
- **Bridge2AI-Voice** (PhysioNet) includes a PD category. As I recall, the public release has features and spectrograms rather than raw audio because of privacy, so check whether it fits your need.
- **Czech and German PD speech corpora** (for example, from Rusz's group in Prague) exist in the literature. They're generally available only through collaboration.

## Common traps
- The **UCI Parkinson's datasets** (Little, Sakar, telemonitoring) have precomputed features only, so they don't meet your requirement. Most Kaggle "Parkinson's voice" datasets are copies of these.
- Most of these are small, with fewer than 100 subjects. For deep learning, split by speaker, never by recording. Otherwise the same speaker lands in both train and test, and your accuracy will be inflated.
- Recording conditions, language and device differ between datasets. A model can learn to identify the dataset or microphone instead of the disease, so avoid training on PD from one source and controls from another.
- Pretrained audio encoders (wav2vec 2.0, HuBERT, Whisper) fine-tuned or used as feature extractors tend to work better than training from scratch at this scale.

I can search the web to confirm the current links and access requirements for any of these. I can also help set up a speaker-independent split once you've picked a dataset.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
I'm working from memory and haven't checked any of these links today, so confirm access terms on each landing page. These are the ones I know of that include raw audio.

**Open download**

| Dataset | Contents | Access |
|---|---|---|
| **MDVR-KCL** (Mobile Device Voice Recordings at King's College London) | About 16 PD and 21 healthy controls. Smartphone WAVs of reading and spontaneous speech. | Zenodo (record ~2867216), open access, no approval needed. |
| **Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech** (Dimauro & Girardi) | About 65 speakers (28 PD, rest healthy controls). Vowels, words, and phrases. | IEEE DataPort (DOI ~10.21227/aw6b-tg17). Free account required, and I believe it's open access. |

**Registration or application required**

| Dataset | Contents | Access |
|---|---|---|
| **mPower** (Sage Bionetworks) | Thousands of participants. 10-second smartphone "aaah" recordings. | Synapse. Register, complete the qualified-researcher steps, and accept the data use terms. The "controls" are self-reported, so labels are noisy. |
| **PC-GITA** (Colombian Spanish, GITA group, Univ. of Antioquia) | 50 PD and 50 age/sex-matched controls. Vowels, DDK, words, sentences, and monologue. | Request from the authors under a license agreement. It's widely used, so it's good for benchmarking. |
| **Neurovoz** (Castilian Spanish) | About 47 PD and 32 controls. Sentences, DDK, and monologues. | Request through the authors' Zenodo/paper page. I'm not sure of the current terms. |
| **Bridge2AI-Voice** (PhysioNet) | Multi-disorder voice data that includes PD. | Credentialed PhysioNet access with a data use agreement. As far as I know, the public releases contain derived features such as spectrograms and MFCCs rather than raw audio, so check whether that fits your needs. |

**Features only, so not what you need:** the Oxford Parkinson's Detection dataset (Little) and the UCI Parkinson Speech datasets (Sakar). They show up in most searches, but they're CSV tables.

**Caveats for deep learning**
- **Small speaker counts.** Most sets have tens of speakers. Split by speaker, never by recording, or you'll get leakage and inflated accuracy.
- **Recording-condition confounds.** If PD and control audio differ in device or room (as can happen in mPower), a model can learn the channel instead of the disease. Check this early.
- **Pooling for scale.** Combining corpora is common, but language and task differ across them. Hold out a whole corpus as a test set to see whether your model generalizes.

I can search the web to confirm current links and access terms, or look for more datasets such as Czech, German, or Mandarin corpora. Want me to do either?

**Caveats for deep learning**

- **Recording-condition confounds.** If PD and control audio differ in device or room (as can happen in mPower), a model can learn the channel instead of the disease. Check this early.
- **Pooling for scale.** Combining corpora is common, but language and task differ across them. Hold out a whole corpus as a test set to see whether your model generalizes.

| Dataset | Contents | Access |
|---|---|---|
| **mPower** (Sage Bionetworks) | Thousands of participants. 10-second smartphone "aaah" recordings. | Synapse. Register, complete the qualified-researcher steps, and accept the data use terms. The "controls" are self-reported, so labels are noisy. |
| **PC-GITA** (Colombian Spanish, GITA group, Univ. of Antioquia) | 50 PD and 50 age/sex-matched controls. Vowels, DDK, words, sentences, and monologue. | Request from the authors under a license agreement. It's widely used, so it's good for benchmarking. |

| **Bridge2AI-Voice** (PhysioNet) | Multi-disorder voice data that includes PD. | Credentialed PhysioNet access with a data use agreement. As far as I know, the public releases contain derived features such as spectrograms and MFCCs rather than raw audio, so check whether that fits your needs. |

**Features only, so not what you need:** the Oxford Parkinson's Detection dataset (Little) and the UCI Parkinson Speech datasets (Sakar). They show up in most searches, but they're CSV tables.

**Caveats for deep learning**
- **Small speaker counts.** Most sets have tens of speakers. Split by speaker, never by recording, or you'll get leakage and inflated accuracy.
- **Recording-condition confounds.** If PD and control audio differ in device or room (as can happen in mPower), a model can learn the channel instead of the disease. Check this early.
- **Pooling for scale.** Combining corpora is common, but language and task differ across them. Hold out a whole corpus as a test set to see whether your model generalizes.

03-recall-mpowerllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the mPower dataset (also known as the Parkinson mPower mobile study from Sage Bionetworks, distributed through Synapse, whose voice activity contains smartphone recordings of a sustained "aaah") as a candidate source of voice recordings from people with Parkinson's disease and controls. FAIL if the answer does not mention mPower, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

04-recall-bridge2ai-voicellm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the Bridge2AI-Voice dataset (also known as B2AI-Voice, the Bridge2AI Voice adult dataset, published on PhysioNet as b2ai-voice, which includes participants with Parkinson's disease) as a candidate dataset for voice-based Parkinson's detection. Listing it with the caveat that the public PhysioNet release holds only derived features and that the raw audio needs a separate request counts as listing it. FAIL if the answer does not mention Bridge2AI-Voice, or dismisses it as having no audio at all without saying that the raw recordings can be requested.

05-access-mdvr-kcl-openllm

PASS if the final answer states that MDVR-KCL (Mobile Device Voice Recordings at King's College London) can be downloaded openly from Zenodo without an application or approval. If the answer names its licence, the licence must be Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 (CC BY 4.0). FAIL if the answer does not mention MDVR-KCL, says it must be requested or approved before download, or gives a different licence (for example a non-commercial one).

06-access-mpower-qualified-researcherllm

PASS if the final answer states that mPower (the Parkinson mPower study data on Synapse) is not an open download but is released only to approved, qualified researchers through Synapse, and mentions at least one of the required steps: registering a Synapse account, passing the Synapse certification quiz, validating the user profile (for example with ORCID and an identity attestation letter), submitting an Intended Data Use statement, or agreeing to the conditions for use (which forbid redistribution). FAIL if the answer does not mention mPower, describes it as freely downloadable, or says nothing about how access to it is obtained.

07-access-bridge2ai-no-raw-audiollm

PASS if the final answer states that the Bridge2AI-Voice dataset released on PhysioNet does not contain the raw audio recordings, only derived data such as spectrograms, MFCCs or other acoustic features. Adding that raw audio requires a separate, more involved request (institutional sign-off through the project's data access committee, with data delivered via Synapse) is welcome but not required. FAIL if the answer does not mention Bridge2AI-Voice, presents its PhysioNet release as providing the raw audio files, or recommends it as a source of audio without saying that the raw recordings are not in the standard release.

08-trap-uci-parkinsons-features-onlyllm

PASS if the final answer does not present the UCI Machine Learning Repository "Parkinsons" dataset (also called the Oxford Parkinson's Disease Detection Dataset by Max Little, 2007: 195 sustained-phonation recordings from 31 people, given as a CSV of 22 voice measures such as jitter, shimmer and HNR) as a source of raw audio recordings. Not mentioning it passes. Mentioning it while saying that it contains only precomputed acoustic features and no audio files passes. FAIL if the answer recommends or lists this UCI dataset as providing the audio recordings the user asked for, or lists it as a candidate without saying that it has no audio.
